#2874. Retirees face a looming deadline for required withdrawals — missing it could trigger a tax penalty

Every year, millions of Americans must take required withdrawals from retirement accounts by Dec. 31.

Amid the busy holiday season, it's easy to miss the year-end deadline for required minimum distributions — a mistake that can trigger a hefty IRS penalty, experts said.

"One of the most common RMD mistakes is waiting until the last minute," said certified financial planner Alex Canellopoulos, director of wealth at Vista Capital Partners in Portland, Oregon.

Most retirees must start RMDs from pretax accounts at age 73. The withdrawal is based on the previous year's account balances on Dec. 31, your age and an IRS "life expectancy factor."

The first annual RMD deadline is April 1 of the year after you turn 73, and future withdrawals are due by Dec. 31. If you wait until April 1 for the first one, you'll need two RMDs that year.

RMDs also apply to certain inherited IRAs if the original IRA owner reached RMD age before their death.

In 2023, more than 14.8 million taxpayers age 70 or older had a traditional individual retirement account, according to the latest IRS data. The average year-end market value of the accounts was about $330,000.

Deferring RMDs until the last minute raises the likelihood of mistakes, such as overlooking certain retirement accounts or making withdrawal calculation errors, Canellopoulos said.

Some 7% of Vanguard investors at RMD age missed their yearly withdrawal in 2025, up slightly from 6.7% in 2024, according to a Vanguard analysis of about 400,000 RMD-age retirees with IRA balances published in December 2025.

Among those investors, the average missed RMD was $10,500 in 2025, Vanguard told CNBC.

Investors who don't take RMDs by the annual deadline may owe an excise tax to the IRS, worth up to 25% of the amount they should have withdrawn. For example, the excise tax could be up to $2,625 for a $10,500 missed RMD.

If investors miss a required withdrawal, they could reduce the penalty to 10% if they "timely corrected" the RMD within two years and file Form 5329, according to the IRS.

In some cases, the IRS could waive the penalty entirely due to "reasonable error" if investors take "reasonable steps" to fix the shortfall, according to the agency. Investors can file Form 5329 with a letter of explanation to request the waiver.

"If you don't ask, you're not going to get it," said certified public accountant Tim Steffen, director of advanced planning at financial services firm Baird. "I've never heard of anybody being denied the waiver."

Investors who give to charity may be skipping a specific donation strategy that can reduce or eliminate their annual RMDs, according to Lawrence Pon, a CFP and CPA based in Redwood City, California.

They can use a so-called qualified charitable distribution, or QCD, Pon said.

A QCD has a dual tax benefit: It doesn't count as taxable income and may also satisfy at least part of an investor's yearly RMD, Pon said.

The move requires a direct transfer from an investor's pretax IRA to an eligible nonprofit organization. They can donate up to $111,000 in 2026. While QCD eligibility begins at age 70½, transfers won't start counting toward RMDs until an investor reaches their RMD age.

Generally, taxpayers only get a tax break for large charitable donations if they itemize deductions on their tax returns. But QCDs still provide a tax benefit for charitable gifts when investors use the standard deduction.

QCDs "provide a real benefit if you are not itemizing [tax deductions]," said Pon.

While there's no tax deduction for QCDs, the transfer won't increase a taxpayer's adjusted gross income, or AGI. Raising AGI can cause other tax consequences, such as higher premiums for Medicare Part B and Part D.

#2871. Steelers need more from DK Metcalf, other highly paid players

The Pittsburgh Steelers are 6-3 and still well positioned to make the NFL playoffs because of a favorable schedule.

For that reason, the 38-18 loss to the Cincinnati Bengals on Nov. 15 was not a total disaster and could plausibly be written off as just a bad day against a red-hot offense led by leading MVP candidate Joe Burrow.

At least that might be true if the team's most highly paid players were producing at the levels they're paid to. They decidedly are not, and until they are, then the Steelers still profile as an upper middle class AFC team capable of reaching the postseason, but probably not winning once they get there.

Required reading: Steelers' veteran CB was torched by Joe Burrow in loss to Bengals: PFF grades

Receiver DK Metcalf, who's in Year 2 of a five-year, $150 million deal, is the chief offender. Through nine games, he's on pace for the least productive season of his eight-year NFL career. It would break the lower threshold that he established last just year, his first in Pittsburgh, when he had 850 yards.

Pro Football Focus data faults him for six drops, a figure that feels generous given how frequently he fails to come down with catchable balls from quarterback Aaron Rodgers.

The contested catch numbers encapsulate the profound struggles better. PFF credits him with receptions on just 10 of 25 opportunities in 2026. That's a 40% contested catch rate, which is the worst in the NFL among receivers with at least that many opportunities. Only eight out of 38 receivers with at least 50 targets are worse overall.

Within that cohort, Metcalf ranks 33rd in yards per route run at 1.24; 32nd in yards after the catch with 125; and dead last in raw catch percentage when targeted at 50.7%.

By no metric has he been the reliable No. 1 option that he's being paid to be. Granted, it's certainly not helped that key offseason acquisition Michael Pittman Jr. hasn't been around much to draw coverage to the opposite side of the field. Still, for $150 million, you expect a player to be able to transcend his circumstances more often than Metcalf has.

He finished the game against the Bengals with 54 yards and a touchdown but was blanked before the game got out of hand. He simply hasn't been good enough.

Linebacker/pass rusher T.J. Watt is also worthy of scrutiny. In two games against Cincinnati this season, the Steelers' $42 million man this season generated zero pressures on Burrow. Not just zero sacks. No hurries, and no quarterback hits. Needless to say, that's not good when Burrow is the man he's primarily meant to disrupt.

Four of his eight sacks this season came in the first two games. And he's on pace for the fewest tackles of his career with nine total so far, meaning he's contributing little unless he's getting to the quarterback.

There are certainly games that he's taken over. His interception return for a touchdown sealed the Week 1 win against Atlanta. His two sacks against Cleveland in Week 8 helped give his team the outright division lead momentarily.

PFF, however, has graded him at 63.8/100 or worse in five of his last seven games. The consistency is nowhere close to where it was when he was winning Defensive Player of the Year honors and setting the NFL sack record.

Beyond those two, you can find fault in a lot of the more highly paid players' respective games.

Tight end Pat Freiermuth has often been invisible as a receiving target with Pittman out because of injury. He's caught just two passes in each of the past four games, and his production against Cincinnati came mostly at garbage time.

Pittman, obviously, hasn't been available much at all because of his foot injuries. He's the offense's third-highest paid player behind Metcalf and Rodgers and has just seven catches for 89 yards in three games.

Linebacker Alex Highsmith, has just one half sack in the past seven games. His cap hit is $17 million in 2026. That problem compounds itself, too, because that lack of production will likely hurt his trade value in the offseason, when the Steelers might be looking to unload him because of big commitments to Watt and fellow outside backer Nick Herbig.

And Rodgers himself was not sharp at all against Cincinnati. He didn't complete a pass for most of the first half. He'd thrown just two touchdowns in regulation in the previous three games. And his two scoring tosses against the Bengals came once the game was well out of reach. He's in a definitive slump himself.

Add it all up, and the Steelers are relying more heavily on their depth than they probably should be in Year 1 of the transition to Mike McCarthy as coach.

If this franchise is going to win its first playoff game since 2016 come January, the guys making the most need to begin playing like it. Otherwise, another disappointment like Sunday night's could be the start of a long winter once again.

#2870. Lakers win after playing bad basketball. Now comes real test

LOS ANGELES – Walker Kessler let out a sigh before taking his seat at the podium at Crypto.com Arena on Friday, Nov. 20.

As much of a cliche as that saying is, it still somehow manages to encapsulate the night – or the whole week, actually – almost perfectly as the Los Angeles Lakers struggled for the third night in a row against another subpar team at home.

It took a dramatic rally and a career night from Quentin Grimes to force overtime and survive the Charlotte Hornets with a win on Tuesday. They simply looked overpowered and had no answer for the Utah Jazz on the second night of a back-to-back, losing by 12 and giving the Jazz just their third win of the season. This time, they found themselves trailing the lowly Sacramento Kings by eight at the half after sleepwalking through the first two quarters.

Head coach JJ Redick took it upon himself to motivate his players. Nobody would repeat what exactly was said but whatever it was, the messaging was received as the Lakers came back to escape yet again with a 106-104 win over the Kings.

"I can't repeat every word I used," Redick said. "The main message was we gotta be tougher, we gotta bring a little bit of energy. Certain nights, whether it's the team, how we're playing, the atmosphere in the arena, you could just be a little flat, and I thought we just had to be tougher and pick up our energy.

The Lakers' big man struggled for most of the first half, picking up four fouls that limited him to just under 12 minutes through the first two quarters. In that time, he had made just three of his six shots -- all of which came under the basket -- lacking touch on multiple finger rolls and just one rebound. Kessler said Redick laid into him in the locker room at halftime. He wasn't being physical enough. He wasn't looking to dunk the ball and was missing some touch around the rim. Defensively, he was playing undisciplined, jumping at pump fakes.

"He lit me up a little bit, and rightfully so," Kessler said. "... I needed it."

The Lakers, and Kessler, responded. They outscored the Kings 52-42 in the second half behind nine points and six rebounds from Kessler, who finished the night with a total of 15 and seven and got his plus-minus up to +4 from the -2 he had at the half.

He shot four of five from the floor in the second half, none bigger than at the start of the fourth quarter.

Collin Sexton had the ball above the three-point line, slashed to his right into the paint and dished it to his former Jazz teammate standing in the corner. Kessler put it up.

"It was a very unorganized play," Redick said. "I'm not even sure why he was standing in the corner."

The ball went through the hoop for Kessler's first three-pointer of the season on his first attempt.

"It was open," Kessler said of his reasoning for taking the shot. "I'm confident in that shot. It was open, and Bull actually told me to space because he was about to slash. ... Shot it. Went in. I feel good. I should've made those free throws (at the end), but that's part of it. It was good."

But despite the win, the Lakers' offensive struggles this week continued. Dončić had another inconsistent night shooting 3-for-8 in the second half after putting up 17 points in the first half. The team collectively shot just 35.1% from three-point range. They turned the ball over 16 times and failed to capitalize on their own takeaways early on.

Nevertheless, the Lakers took home their 14th win of the year.

"Obviously we started out not good at all," Kessler said. Me personally, I didn't play well in the first half, but I like how we responded. There's sometimes a tendency to lay down and just completely die. So it was good to see the guys fight through another tough game."

It doesn't get easier from here. The Lakers struggled against the two worst teams in the Western Conference on Wednesday and Friday. They'll have a chance to close out their homestand with a rematch against Utah on Monday before embarking on a four-game road trip that includes stops against the Memphis Grizzlies (12-4), San Antonio Spurs (13-3) and Houston Rockets (12-5).

It's not uncommon for teams to fall into the trap of playing down to the level of their competition. In a league as talented as the NBA, that can lead to some odd stretches like the one the Lakers have been on over the past week. But the team's critics have been quick to point at a perceived "soft" schedule as the driving factor behind their red-hot start that has gotten them to the first seed in the West through 17 games. They'll use these last three as evidence to support their argument, too.

The next five, however, might give a little more insight into where the Lakers really stand among their peers at this point in the season.

It's a small sample size and, in the end, the Lakers won two out of their last three games, but there is little doubt that the team just did not play up to its usual standards in the past week. #2868. What's ahead for AI financing after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang departs

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's departure hasn't produced a broad shock to the credit market, but it is putting the company's role as a financier of artificial intelligence infrastructure under scrutiny.

Huang, who stepped down Thursday amid an espionage and adultery scandal, built Nvidia into an AI powerhouse in the 33 years since he co-founded the company. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO as the company searches for a permanent chief executive.

Nvidia not only supplies its graphics processing units to hyperscalers and tech giants, it also provides financing to other companies to help the industry expand. That could all change.

"Although we expect the company to fulfill all of its previously announced commitments, we believe that going forward, it may be more difficult for the company to negotiate new multi-party investments among ecosystem partners at the same scale it has done up to this point," Goldman Sachs analyst James Schneider said in a note Thursday.

The news of Huang's resignation comes after investors were already concerned about the massive amount of debt being taken on to fund AI infrastructure.

Tech bond yields have risen and spreads have widened on credit default swaps, most notably in lower-rated names. Credit default swaps are contracts in which investors pay for protection in case the borrower can't repay its debt. Wider spreads indicate investors view the bonds as riskier.

On Friday, the yield on Nvidia's 4.5% bond due in 2031 sat at 5.717%, nearly 5% higher week to date. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

Tech stocks slumped Friday with the Nasdaq 100 closing down 1.2% as markets continued to digest the Nvidia news. Yields on hyperscaler bonds ticked slightly higher on Friday, signaling the broader concern about AI debt lingers.

It's the smaller AI companies that could ultimately see an impact if Nvidia makes any changes to its AI financing. The company had $36 billion in off-balance-sheet AI cloud agreements, or backstops, for neoclouds, according to its last quarterly financial filing in July.

Neoclouds CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all in Nvidia's portfolio. Their shares were down sharply Thursday.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child for AI debt fears, saw its bond yields retreat after its quarterly earnings on Tuesday eased investor concerns. The yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 14.79% on Friday after climbing to 16.7% last week. The spread on its five-year CDS also dropped more than 17% week to date, although it remained above 1,000 — which is generally considered a sign of distress.

It remains unclear how Nvidia's support for those companies may change, said CreditSights analyst Andy Li.

"We don't know what the ultimate strategy for Nvidia is going to be coming out the other side," he said.

"However, I think directionally, just given the fact that Jensen was so crucial in selling the Nvidia story and being a very aggressive deal maker, it's likely that a lot of this creative deal-making and circular financing and equity and credit support is going to moderate," he added.

CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator told CNBC Wednesday, before Huang's departure, that Nvidia "is not one person."

"There are thousands of people over there that interface with thousands of people at my company every day. They integrate on the technology. They integrate on the physical infrastructure. They integrate on the networking," he said in an interview with "Squawk on the Street." "They have been a great partner for us for years. They will continue to be a great partner for us."

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, doesn't see any big changes in terms of Nvidia's relevance or its ability to finance for now. As CFO, Kress was involved in the financing deals, and the search for a permanent CEO is going to take some time, he noted.

The real issue is just how much money the company will want to lend for AI financing, rather than, say using its hundreds of billions in free cash flow for other purposes, like share buybacks, capex, or acquisitions.

"Big companies like Nvidia and Meta, they're holding all the cards," he said. "When they decide that the game is over, that's it — it's going to be curtains for some of these smaller companies."

— CNBC'S Kif Leswing and Isabel O'Brien contributed reporting.

#2866. Roob's Observations: Why on Earth is Johnny Mundt playing more than Eli Stowers?

Roob's Observations: Why on Earth is Johnny Mundt playing more than Eli Stowers? originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

When you've lost seven in a row, there are countless questions to answer. For this Eagles team, they include: Why is Johnny Mundt playing more than Eli Stowers? What should the Eagles do with their top-10 draft pick in April? How will they replace Jordan Davis's snaps? How lackluster has the Eagles' passing game been?

We'll do our best to answer these and a number of other pressing questions facing the hapless Eagles, who are 2-7 for only the third time since 1975.

1. Someone has to explain to me why Johnny Mundt is playing more than Eli Stowers. Mundt is a 10-year veteran who has four catches for 16 yards this year and has not been close to the blocker we all expected when the Eagles signed him. For literally zero value, Mundt has played a ridiculous 220 snaps, or 24 ½ per game. Why? He brings nothing to the table. Then there's Stowers, a promising rookie who is finally healthy after dealing with a lingering hamstring injury. The Eagles invested a 2nd-round pick on Stowers, yet he's buried on the bench. In the three games since he came back healthy, Mundt has played 37 snaps and Stowers has played 32. There is absolutely no reason on Earth a 31-year-old journeyman with no receiving ability and minimal blocking prowess should be playing more than a 23-year-old rookie 2nd-round pick. None. Even if Mundt was good, the Eagles are 2-7 and this is a time to see what the young guys can do and get them as many reps as possible. Stowers got a few targets late in the Washington game and caught three passes for 53 yards, including a 34-yarder. He had more yards on that one 34-yard catch than Mundt has all year. And do you know when Mundt's last 34-yard catch was? It was in 2020. Six years and four teams ago. You're 2-7. What are we doing here? Play the young guys. Especially ones who have shown some promise. I don't know if Stowers will ever be a legit NFL tight end. But I do know we're not going to find out if he's not playing.

2. This is amazing to me: From 1986 through 1997 – over parts of 12 seasons – the Eagles went 26-0 when they had a 100-yard rusher. Randall Cunningham ran for 110 yards in a 13-11 loss to the Lions at the Vet in November 1986 and the Eagles didn't lose again with a 100-yard rusher until September of 1997, when Ricky Watters ran for 106 yards in a 21-20 loss to the Cowboys at Texas Stadium. They also had a 10-game winning streak with a 100-yard rusher from 2002 through 2005, a 13-game winning streak with a 100-yard rusher from 2013 through 2016 and a 19-game winning streak from 2020 through 2024. In all, since 1987 they're 114-24 with a 100-yard rusher. They're 27-3 in their last 30 games with a 100-yard rusher. That's totally nuts. This year? They're 0-0. No 100-yard rushers since last year.

3. Two of the bigger decisions the Eagles will have this offseason are interlocked. What to do with Jalen Hurts and what to do with what will likely be a top-10 pick – and quite possibly a top-5 pick. Let's operate under the assumption that Hurts will be healthy by next season and able to play. And for the purpose of this conversation let's also  assume the Eagles figure out a way to sign him to an extension so he's not going into the final year of guaranteed money in 2027. Because where are you going to find a better quarterback than a guy who took the team to two Super Bowls, winning one, has won 68 percent of his career starts and reached the playoffs in every full season he's played? Might be tricky to determine his value coming off this lost season, but Howie is Howie. He'll figure it out. With those two assumptions, the question becomes what do the Eagles do with such a premium draft pick if they're not going to take a quarterback, and I think it comes down to edge rusher or offensive tackle. And you can make a pretty good case for edge, and this draft is loaded at edge, I would go offensive tackle. Unless the Eagles see Markel Bell as the long-term replacement for Lane Johnson, which I doubt will be the case, that's priority No. 1. This offensive line is just a train wreck right now and Roseman's biggest offseason assignment is going to be rebuilding it. That may start with the coach, but it's got to include personnel, and if you're picking high enough to land a Jordan Seaton from LSU or Trevor Goosby of Texas that's a heck of a place to start. There's also the Jaguars' 1st-round pick, but that will obviously be way later than the Eagles' own, probably late enough to be a best-player-available pick. The Eagles have such a tradition of elite offensive tackles, with Jon Runyan, Tra Thomas, Jason Peters, Lane Johnson and Jordan Mailata. Time to find the next guy in the lineage.

4. With Jordan Davis no longer here, one guy I want to see more of is Ty Robinson, last year's 4th-round pick. Ty had a very good second half of training camp after a slow start but has only played 61 snaps, or about 6 ½ per game. I want to see him play. I don't need to see more Byron Young. He's played 19 snaps per game and barely caused a ripple. I've seen more out of Robinson in about a third the snaps. Davis averaged 36 snaps per game, and Jalen Carter is already playing 84 percent of the snaps so he can't play more. But I'd expect Moro Ojomo – who's at 60 percent and 42 snaps per game – to add about 10 snaps a game and Young to add maybe five and then whoever is the fifth guy – practice squad call-up Leki Fotu or Gabe Hall – is going to play about 10 snaps a game. So that should leave maybe a dozen more snaps for Robinson. I like the player and want to see more of him.

5. Here's a stat that really illustrates how lifeless the Eagles' passing game has been. The Eagles are averaging 9.7 yards per completion, which would equal the lowest in franchise history over a full season. They also averaged 9.7 in 1998. The only other year they were under 10 is 1974, when they finished at 9.8. The Eagles are last in the NFL with just 14 pass plays of 20 yards or more. Only five of those in the second half. Their last 2nd-half completion of 20 yards to a WR was in Week 2, when Jalen Hurts had a 20-yarder to Dontayvion Wicks and a 23-yarder to Darius Cooper. Man, this offense is just brutal.

6. Quez Watkins caught a 26-yard pass from Daniel Jones Thursday night in the Colts' loss to the Texans, and I know people around here aren't particulary fond of Quez and I get it. His Super Bowl drop was inexcusable. His fumble after a 51-yard catch cost the Eagles a win against the Commanders. His refusal to go hard for the ball cost Gardner Minshew two interceptions in a game against the Cowboys. His last few years here were a disaster. I get all that. But here's the thing. I'm just a fan of guys who don't give up no matter how extreme the odds, and Watkins never gave up on his NFL dream despite not playing a snap in 2024 or 2025 or the first half of 2026 and getting released by four teams. This is a dude who was a 6th-round pick in 2020 and had a decent year in 2021 with 43-for-647 before his career began to spiral downhill. The Eagles even brought him back last year on the practice squad for a bit, and despite all the high-leverage mistakes I found that he had a great attitude when I chatted with him after he got here in September: "Yeah, the last couple years have just been very stressful for me," Watkins said at his locker. "But at the same time, I'm still pushing, still going and trying to get back to where I was. I definitely have a lot of football left. It's just a matter of when. When my time comes, when my time comes back around, I'll be ready to ball again." The 26-yarder Thursday night was his longest catch since a 30-yard touchdown from Jalen Hurts in a win over the Packers at the Linc in November 2022. Four years ago. Easy to dislike a guy like Quez Watkins, and I don't blame anyone who does. Me, I can't help admiring his determination and resilience. He's a survivor, and in the NFL that's not an easy thing to be.

7. Joe Milton had a pretty funny answer when asked about the nickname Bazooka Joe, which cleverly combines his first name with a metaphor for his massive arm. "I don't like it," he said. Why not? "Because bazookas only travel so far." What does he prefer? "Mr. Rocket."  You might remember that Randall Cunningham told the beat guys after the 1995 playoff loss in Dallas – his last game as an Eagle – that he nearly named his newborn son "Johnny Rocket." Randall put four names into a hat including "Randall Jr." and "Johnny Rocket," and his wife Felicity – fortunately – picked Randall Jr., born in Las Vegas the day before that 30-11 loss, went on to become a two-time NCAA Division 1 high jump champ for USC. And Randall didn't play again for two years, until Brian Billick and the Vikings revived his career.

8. Sixteen of the 27 all-time field goals of 55 yards or longer against the Eagles – 59 percent – have been made since Nick Sirianni became head coach. Kickers have made more field goals of 55 yards against the Eagles this year (five) than the first 77 years of the team's existence (four). From 1933 through 2017, opposing kickers were 6-for-23 against the Eagles from 55 yards and out (26 percent). Since 2018, they're 21-for-28 (75 percent). Ten of the 27 55-yarders against the Eagles have come since opening day last year.

9. How wild is it that the Patriots' leading receiver who's a former Eagle isn't A.J. Brown but Mack Hollins, still going strong in his 10th NFL season. Hollins, the Eagles' 4th-round pick in 2017, missed three games this year but has 23 catches for 293 yards in six games compared to Brown's 11-for-128 in four games. Hollins, who won a Super Bowl ring with the Eagles as a rookie, has really had a remarkable career with six teams, catching 231 passes for 2,912 yards and 18 touchdowns after a very slow first few years. He's 33 now and has the 4th-most catches ever by a WR after his 32nd birthday drafted by the Eagles behind Hall of Famers Harold Carmichael and Tommy McDonald plus DeSean Jackson. Good ol' Mack Hollins. Still at it.

10. When Kyle McCord started for the Dolphins against the Colts Sunday, he became only the fifth quarterback drafted by the Eagles in the sixth round or later to start an NFL regular-season game. The first was Dick Jamieson, the Eagles' 25th-round pick out of Bradley in 1959. He started only one game in the NFL, for the New York Titans in 1960 and won it, 27-3 over the Bills at the Polo Grounds. Bob Berry – the Eagles' 11th-round pick out of Oregon in 1964 – started 52 games with the Vikings and Falcons from 1966 through 1974. Koy Detmer, a 7th-round pick in 1997 out of Colorado, went 3-5 in eight career starts with the Eagles, and Tanner McKee, a 6th-round pick out of Stanford in 2024, is now 1-4 in five career starts. The Eagles have only drafted 22 quarterbacks in franchise history who have started an NFL game. Of the 49 quarterbacks who've started a game for the Eagles, only 14 were drafted by the Eagles.

#2865. Wales coach Tandy frustrated by Australia defeat

Wales head coach Steve Tandy said he was frustrated as his side failed to back up their epic win over New Zealand, narrowly losing to Australia.

The Wallabies edged the Nations Championship Test 29-26 in Cardiff, a week after a first Wales victory over the All Blacks since 1953 had led to wild celebrations.

Taniela Tupou, Brandon Paenga-Amosa, Max Jorgensen and Len Ikitau secured a try-bonus point for Australia as Ryan Lonergan and Carter Gordon added nine points with the boot.

Wales also scored four tries, through Dewi Lake's double, Blair Murray and Ryan Elias, with Sam Costelow landing three conversions.

"The overarching feeling is the frustration of losing," said Tandy.

"We were disappointed. We probably weren't as accurate as we could have been. We were held up three times and could have gone ahead on the scoreboard."

Tandy added: "Maybe we needed a little bit of composure but there were enough opportunities to come away with the win.

"I know it's not the result anyone wanted but the supporters were amazing again."

Despite the loss, and that frustration, the Wales head coach praised his side's attitude in attempting to follow up their historic win against the All Blacks.

"Fair play to the boys, after such a monumental game last week with the emotional energy, we stuck in the game and scored four tries," he said.

"So there are a lot of positives in the performance to take as well."

Australia hold on for narrow win against Wales

Nations Championship - Australia triumph over Wales - reaction

Dewi Lake has scored three tries in the past two internationals [Huw Evans Picture Agency]

Australia, having soaked up fierce first-half pressure, produced some fine attacking rugby after the break to prevent Wales from winning three matches in a row for the first time since the 2023 World Cup.

"It's sport, you have major highs and then lows, and that's the game we play," said skipper Lake.

"Our frustration comes from the fact that we probably weren't at our best.

"We created opportunities during large parts of the game, but there were little inaccuracies at the wrong times."

Lake, however, said he could not fault the effort of the squad.

"We spoke about backing up last weekend, and we didn't back it up with a result but I think we backed it up with effort and energy," said the captain.

"We had opportunities to win that game, but unfortunately it wasn't to be for us today.

"We'll fix those accuracy issues and we'll be ready to go again next week."

Wales will face Japan at the Allianz Stadium at Twickenham on Saturday after both sides finished fifth in the two Nations Championship tables - north and south.

It will be a fifth time Wales and Japan have met in 16 months with Tandy's side defeating Eddie Jones' team 28-21 in Cardiff just two weeks ago.

Wales have an injury doubt over scrum-half Tomos Williams who has a tight groin, with Tandy expected to make a number changes to freshen up the side.

Australia head coach Les Kiss was happy to have broken a four-match losing sequence which included recent defeats against Scotland and England.

"I am not only proud but also happy for these boys," said Kiss.

"The work they've been putting in over the last four weeks has been good and they just haven't been getting a reward

"Hats off to Wales. They beat the All Blacks here last week but didn't seem to lack any verve or energy.

"That didn't take it out of them and actually created more energy and confidence in them."

Captain Harry Wilson said it was a special to end the Wallabies poor sequence.

"When you have four losses on the bounce, you question a few things," said the back-row forward.

"Rugby is such a results-driven game and we hadn't been getting the results even though I felt like we weren't far off last week

"To beat a team who just had defeated the All Blacks, in front of 65,000 Wales supporters where the atmosphere was crazy, was special."

The Wallabies will now face Ireland on Friday in the contest for fifth place in Twickenham (20:10 GMT) after both sides finished third in their respective pools.

#2864. Nvidia stock plunges 14%, wraps up longest weekly losing streak since 2008 financial crisis

The last time Wall Street was this down on Nvidia the chipmaker's flagship product was the GeForce graphics processor for high-end PC video games, and markets were reeling from the global financial crisis.

Nvidia shares tumbled 14% on Friday, sliding for a seventh straight day as investors grew increasingly concerned about life after co-founder Jensen Huang. The stock closed lower for a seventh consecutive week, the longest such stretch since 2008.

Shares of Nvidia are down 46% over the period, pushing the company's market cap down by about $2.6 trillion. The stock is now trading at roughly 8.4 times forward earnings, while Advanced Micro Devices is valued at 29 times profit for the next 12 months.

On Friday, Reuters reported that the Securities and Exchange Commission is exploring potential accounting issues at Nvidia, pushing the stock down further.

Once the most valuable company in the world, Nvidia now has a market cap of $3 trillion, putting it fourth behind Apple, Microsoft and Alphabet.

Nvidia is reeling from the removal of Huang, who resigned on Nov. 12, after multiple reported affairs, including one with a Chinese businesswoman who was allegedly a spy.

Harvey Jones, Nvidia's newly appointed board chair, said earlier this week that Huang had resigned after "disclosure of personal behavior inconsistent with the company's code of conduct, standards and cultural norms."

But the chipmaker's losing streak started before Nvidia was thrown into crisis as investors worried over the sustainability of the AI buildout, particularly after the midterm elections, and emerging competition in AI chips from Google and AMD.

Even a better-than-expected earnings report, which included an increase to fiscal 2028 guidance, didn't stem the selling. Interim CEO Colette Kress, the company's longtime CFO, projected revenue growth next fiscal year of 75%, and said that customers aren't going anywhere.

On Tuesday's earnings call with analysts, Kress signed off by saying, "we've got this." In a company meeting the next day, executives told staff that it was moving its yearly review process, which can come with more stock, from March to December.

For investors, one major question is whether Nvidia can continue to innovate and sustain its commanding lead in the AI chip market without its visionary leader. Almost the entire company now is built around the AI trade, a phenomenon that began taking hold just four years ago with the launch of OpenAI's ChatGPT.

Before that, Nvidia's primary business was selling graphics cards used for video games. In mid-2008, the last time the stock was this deep into a weekly losing streak, Nvidia was generating less than $1 billion per quarter. Now it pulls in more than $1 billion per day. At the time, the entire market was in freefall due to the worst financial crisis since the Great Depression.

#2863. Nick Sirianni sticking with Sean Mannion, Joe Milton out of bye

Nick Sirianni sticking with Sean Mannion, Joe Milton out of bye  originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles are sticking with offensive coordinator Sean Mannion and quarterback Joe Milton coming out of the bye week, head coach Nick Sirianni said on Wednesday afternoon.

Before their Week 10 bye week, the Eagles lost seven straight games with an offense that now ranks dead last in yards and 30th in scoring.

But Sirianni is sticking with Mannion as the Eagles 'play-caller going into Week 11's game against the Steelers.

"I think we had a really good bye week process," Sirianni said. "Got to evaluate a lot of things within that. I think I put into pretty good detail of kind of how we go through things and the things that present themselves as you go through it. I think we have a good plan of action moving forward of the things that we want to do to move forward and to get better. Obviously, we haven't been good enough on offense but I like our plan of attack and our process that we went through last week."

There was a lot of excitement when the Eagles hired Mannion this offseason but the offense has struggled all season. Mannion is a first-year OC, so maybe the Eagles expected some growing pains. But this has been extreme. They literally have the worst offense in the NFL. Even when you acknowledge the Eagles lost Jalen Hurts and have been really injured, that's still not acceptable.

Sirianni has previously praised Mannion for his work ethic. But aside from that, what gives the head coach confidence in Mannion?

"I think he's really smart. I think he has a great football mind," Sirianni said. "He thinks through everything. I think one thing I always value about him is anybody can come and give a play and say, 'Hey, I want to do this or I want to do that.' But I think he has a really good monotonous approach to figuring out everything that could potentially happen in that situation and what are the downfalls and what [is] the upside and do we move forward with some of those things? Again, I think that just speaks to the detail of what he has.

"Again, it hasn't went well enough for any of us this year offensively. That's coaches, that's players. We all have things that we need to do better. But I do love that approach that he has with that. As we looked at some of our schemes that he's brought in, some of those have been really, really good. Again, it has to be better as a whole. Without getting too much into the specifics of what those are. We just need to build on that."

The Eagles did recently bring back T.J. Paganetti as a consultant, but Sirianni said he didn't make any other staff changes during the bye week.

The Eagles gave Milton his first-career start before the bye week in the 19-11 loss to the Giants. While Milton did some good things, the offense still struggled, which was probably to be expected after he had been with the team for just three weeks.

Perhaps Milton will be in a better spot against the Steelers on Sunday.

"He works extremely hard with everything," Sirianni said of Milton. "Really love his approach, like his personality. But I really appreciate his approach of how he goes through everything. Coaches have done a nice job, Parks (Frazier) has done a nice job. But his approach, he's a really hard worker and I really value that."

The good news for Mannion, Milton and the offense is that Sirianni said the team is "hopeful" that DeVonta Smith (hamstring) and Dontayvion Wicks (ankle) will be able to play on Sunday. The Eagles' top two receivers missed the three games before the bye week.

#2862. Sixers host Milwaukee Bucks for afternoon game

The Philadelphia 76ers are coming off several strong performances, stringing together wins and most recently earning a comeback victory over the Orlando Magic despite missing several key players. Now, they'll return to Philly to host the Milwaukee Bucks for an early afternoon affair.

The Bucks, as things stand, sit in the basement of the Eastern Conference with a 1-15 record. Fresh off trading Giannis Antetokounmpo to Miami, they've kicked off a rebuild focused on players such as Tyler Herro, Jaime Jaquez Jr., Kel'el Ware and Ryan Rollins. They also added Brayden Burries and Nate Ament as two lottery picks in a stacked draft, and now they're looking to right the ship following the departure of their franchise star.

As their record suggests, things have not gone smoothly. Myles Turner, who remains with the team, has also endured a rough start to the season amid the turmoil. While all of the young players mentioned above have shown flashes of their potential, the Bucks are still far from being a consistent, winning basketball team.

The Sixers, on the other hand, are finding their groove and sit atop the Eastern Conference. They're coming off a strong win, with several of their key players filling up the box score. LeBron James has begun to find his rhythm, while Joel Embiid has played a crucial role in several of their recent victories. VJ Edgecombe, playing without Tyrese Maxey in their last game, was instrumental in their win over Orlando, hitting what ultimately proved to be the game-winning shot while making his presence felt on both ends of the floor.

There are a few player statuses to monitor heading into this one, but the sophomore has proven time and time again that he can step up and hit big shots when called upon. The same can be said for the Sixers' backcourt rotation, which also features Anfernee Simons and rookie Labaron Philon. Philon saw meaningful minutes in their last game and posted solid numbers in limited playing time.

Even Tacko Fall logged meaningful minutes, providing a crucial presence in the frontcourt. The Bucks have no shortage of frontcourt players to throw at the Sixers, and this could be another contest where Fall has an opportunity to solidify his role amid the team's myriad injuries.

Speaking of injuries, there are a few to monitor heading into this game. Jaylen Brown will miss the matchup as he recovers from a hamstring strain, while Maxey is listed as questionable with a foot strain. Dominick Barlow is probable and could make his season debut, while Adem Bona and Dean Wade remain out. For Milwaukee, Ousmane Dieng is also out.

All of this presents a great opportunity for the Sixers to extend their winning streak against a less talented opponent. Games like this won't come often on their schedule, and they'd be wise to take advantage. Early afternoon games can always get weird quickly, but the Sixers have a golden opportunity to add another win and maintain their early-season momentum. Let's see if they can get it done.

#2861. How China is using summits to expand its global influence

SHENZHEN, China — For a second year in a row, Chinese President Xi Jinping has brought Russian President Vladimir Putin together with another major world leader on mainland Chinese shores.

U.S. President Donald Trump had a trilateral meeting with Xi and Putin on Thursday, after the three met the prior evening during a welcome banquet for the Asia-Pacific Economic Leaders' Meeting in Shenzhen. It marked the first time they had met together in person.

In September 2025, Xi, Putin and Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi briefly laughed together for a friendly photo-op alongside an annual meeting of the Shanghai Cooperation Organization in Tianjin, China. The gathering followed easing tensions between Beijing and New Delhi.

China's "ability to engage directly with the world major powers" reflects the country's influence across the Global South and Eurasia, said Jodie Wen, postdoctoral fellow at the Center for International Security and Strategy at Tsinghua University. "China hopes to play a more constructive role in this conflict-[ridden], unstable world."

In the future, "China will hope to provide more platforms" within its borders for countries to discuss issues, she said.

Trump, Xi, Putin said they plan to boost oil production, revamp UN after trilateral talks

I watched Trump, Xi and Putin gather in China. Here are my top takeaways

Trump holds talks separately with Putin and Xi ahead of their historic trilateral meeting

Takaichi gets chilly reception in China on first Japan leader visit since 2019

Why the Middle East is watching the Trump-Xi-Putin summit

Trump arrives in China for historic meeting with Xi, Putin

China's Xi calls for removing trade and investment barriers

Putin says EU is losing industry, falling behind competition

Three things to watch as Trump, Xi and Putin meet in China

China has increasingly played the role of broker, for instance, providing the forum for restoring diplomatic ties between Saudi Arabia and Iran in 2023.

By participating in and hosting different multilateral organizations, China has stayed in the Western orbit while cultivating relations with non-Western countries. The two photo-ops last year and this year reflect only a portion of Xi's large number of meetings with different world leaders.

Xi has hosted more than 40 heads of state so far this year, including at least eight around the APEC meetings in Shenzhen this week. That's on top of receiving over 60 state visits in 2025, according to CNBC analysis of official Chinese readouts.

Domestic Chinese media have highlighted how Xi has met with many foreign leaders around the China-hosted events.

"Beijing sees these multilateral platforms as opportunities to expand its international influence and demonstrate its commitment to multilateralism, while bilateral diplomacy remains equally important," said Yue Su, principal economist for China at EIU.

But she noted that different countries increasingly pursue different geopolitical and economic priorities, making it challenging to reach consensus even through multilateral organizations.

"Against this backdrop, China's diplomatic priorities are increasingly focused on delivering tangible economic benefits through trade, investment, financial connectivity and technology cooperation," she said.

On Thursday, Xi announced at the APEC Economic Leaders' Meeting that China would provide $20 million in support for the Asia Pacific region. He also advocated for the development of an "Asia-Pacific community with a shared future," an iteration of his commonly used slogan on a "shared future" for humanity more broadly.

The Shenzhen summit can create meaningful outcomes for crisis management, as well as diplomatic space for future negotiations, Tsinghua University's Wen said. She added that it's important for leaders to come together in person in order to avoid misunderstandings and further tensions.

Trump said Thursday at the trilateral that the U.S., China and Russia are "uniquely positioned" to discuss reducing nuclear weapons at their historic meeting. A Chinese readout subsequently said the three leaders discussed Ukraine, the Middle East and the Korean Peninsula.

—CNBC's Matthew Tan contributed to this report.

#2860. Ravens’ Coach Calls Out Jacoby Brissett Adjustment

Woolfork knows what makes Brissett special for the Ravens.

Making Jacoby Brissett their fourth starting quarterback of the 2026 NFL season is proving quite the adjustment for the Baltimore Ravens, and a key coach called out the process ahead of the game against the Carolina Panthers in Week 11.

Ravens' quarterbacks coach Israel Woolfork knows what it takes to play football's most important position in Baltimore. Woolfork also knows more than most about Brissett, whom he coached with both the Cleveland Browns and the Arizona Cardinals.

It means when Woolfork talks about the Ravens' latest QB1, fans should listen. Those who do will welcome Woolfork telling reporters, including The Baltimore Sun's Sam Cohn, how Brissett's been "the ultimate pro, coming in and being himself. Obviously, as a quarterback, he's one of the biggest leaders on your team, but he's not forcing it. He's coming in. He's putting in his work every single day."

Former New England Patriots and Indianapolis Colts passer Brissett's a well-travelled veteran of stepping into difficult quarterback situations, so his ability to seamlessly acclimate to a new team is hardly surprising. What's more encouraging for the Ravens is what Woolfork noted about Brissett during the latter's physically rough debut against the Los Angeles Chargers on Monday Night Football in Week 10.

The Ravens couldn't win with Brissett at the controls, even against the winless Chargers. Yet, Brissett still did enough during the 23-20 defeat to show why he's a credible fill-in for injured two-time NFL MVP Lamar Jackson and Pro Bowl backup Tyler Huntley.

Brissett put the ball in the air 49 times against the Bolts, but Woolfork still thinks the 33-year-old is a bigger asset in another phase of offense. As Woolfork put it, Brissett's "really good at run action [and] standing in the pocket and delivering. [He's] able to take some hits, wipe himself off and get up and get to the next play," per Geoffrey A. Knox of Ravens Wire.

Those words echo the endorsement of Brissett's skill-set from a former Cardinals starter who's watched the experienced game-manager closely. It's curious Woolfork and others have focused on Brissett's talent for using the run game to his advantage. Especially since Ravens' offensive coordinator Declan Doyle gave running back Derrick Henry just 10 carries last week.

GettyWoolfork knows what makes Brissett special for the Ravens.

Brissett still made good use of the play-action game. He completed 13 play-action passes for 85 yards, according to Pro Football Reference.

The only way to boost those numbers is for the Ravens to change their recent thinking about Henry and the ground attack.

Doyle's been getting trashed by many, including an all-time great, for how he's using, or not using, Henry. This doesn't have to be a complicated issue. Just give No. 22 the ball more often on the plays he runs best.

Henry needs to be trusted to be himself if he's going to get back to his best, but Doyle can still throw some different things at defenses. Different like unleashing capable third-down back Justice Hill on earlier downs.

Hill remains an effective change-of-pace runner, so he could add the extra spark that's been missing while Henry's struggled. A more active share of the carries can get this rushing attack revving again against the 22nd-ranked Panthers' run defense that's allowing 124 yards per game.

Giving the Panthers more of what they can't stop will not only help these Ravens return to play Ravens-style football. It'll also help Brissett accelerate his transition, make good on Woolfork's words and start helping a struggling team win some games again.

This article was originally published on HEAVY

#2858. Eagles vs. Steelers predictions for Week 11 of the 2026 NFL season

Eagles vs. Steelers predictions for Week 11 of the 2026 NFL season originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles (2-7) are back from their bye week and will host the Pittsburgh Steelers (6-3) on Sunday afternoon at the Linc.

Someday this year the Eagles will win another game. I don't think it will be this one. The Steelers do a lot of things well. They're third in the NFL in run defense and seventh in scoring defense, allowing only 20 points per game, and T.J. Watt is still terrorizing quarterbacks at 32 years old. Undrafted Jaylen Warren leads the NFL at 5.7 yards per carry and is a dangerous receiver out of the backfield, future 1st-ballot Hall of Famer Aaron Rodgers can still make plays when he's protected – he's got 15 TD passes and six INTs this year – and D.J. Metcalf isn't what he used to be with Seattle but still capable of coming up big a few times a game. The Steelres are 6-3 and 4-1 in their last five and they don't have a ton of quality wins, but they aren't playing a quality team Sunday afternoon at the Linc. The Steelers aren't a Super Bowl threat, but they do a lot of things well. The Eagles don't do anything well. Except lose. They're good at that. Getting DeVonta Smith back and a healthier Saquon Barkley should help Joe Milton, but I just don't see the Eagles competing for 60 minutes with a solid team like the Steelers.

I don't think this Steelers team is as good as their record but I do think they're better than the Eagles. Even though the bye week should definitely help the Eagles find some better answers on offense as Joe Milton learns the scheme, that Steelers defense is good up front. There are some serious concerns about pass pro holding up in this game and the Steelers are hard to run against. The one advantage the Eagles have is at receiver as DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks return. But Milton will need time to throw. The Eagles' defense should be able to slow down this version of Aaron Rodgers but I just don't have a lot of faith that the Eagles will be able to snap their losing streak.

Last week's bye week was a much-needed break for this Eagles team. Players needed to heal up physically and mentally. As a player, the bye week allowed me to decompress and refocus on the season. Even when the season is going well, getting away helps the mind and body reset for the remainder of the season.

Coaches don't usually get that break. They try to figure out how to get the players performing better, fix what's going wrong and reinforce what they should continue doing right.

On offense, getting DeVonta Smith back will surely boost the passing attack for Joe Milton. Helping the O-line with protection should be the offense's No. 1 focus. Markel Bell will need help against one of the best pass rushers in the league in T.J. Watt. They must send a chipping RB, TE presence and slide the line toward him. Sean Mannion cannot let the right side of the line wreck the entire offense.

Defensively, Vic Fangio will have to call an aggressive style of play. The DBs will have to play tighter coverage or Aaron Rodgers will pick the defense apart. Stopping the run without Jordan Davis will also be a priority. A collective effort will be required to compensate for losing Davis on 1st and 2nd down. Can the Eagles keep the win streak going against the Steelers who have not won in Philadelphia since 1965? Maybe. Will they? Probably not.

Fresh off the bye week with some reinforcements on the way at receiver, you'd have to hope the Eagles offense shows some level of improvement. Against the Giants, they managed just one score and 30 of the 60 yards gained on that drive came via penalty. It has been an abysmal effort of late. Having DeVonta Smith and more time on task for new starting quarterback Joe Milton should — in theory — lead to better results.

Aaron Rodgers turns 43 next month and just lost his top interior protector on the offensive line in Mason McCormick. That should spell a big game for Jalen Carter. He tends to feast on inferior opponents. However, the Eagles are down both of their defensive captains. Zack Baun is dealing with a back injury and Jordan Davis was just dealt to the Jaguars. I suspect they'll struggle to stop the Steelers' rushing attack, led by Jaylen Warren.

The Steelers haven't won a game in Philadelphia since 1965. That changes on Sunday.

The Steelers are much better on paper than the way they are actually playing. Pittsburgh has a great defensive line… that doesn't get a lot of pressure. Talented receivers… to go along with one of the worst passing offenses in the NFL. The stats don't match up to the talent you see on the Steelers team. However, they are good at two things — running the football and winning close games. Two things the Eagles cannot seem to figure out so far this season. I am encouraged to see if the Eagles offense can make any strides with Joe Milton's second game at quarterback and the return of DeVonta Smith and Wicks. The Eagles' defensive line has a winnable matchup against a banged up Steelers offensive line but Aaron Rodgers is known for getting the ball out quick. I don't trust anything the Eagles are doing right now to be able to win this game.

It's good to have football back in the city of Philadelphia. I know it's been a frustrating campaign to say the least, but when football is in town, the city has a different type of energy to it. Against the Steelers, you know exactly the type of ballgame it's going to be. They want to play a slow, monotonous brand of football where they run the ball, churn clock, smother you defensively, and leave town with a win without doing anything spectacular. They're a veteran-laden unit headlined by future Hall of Famers in Aaron Rodgers and T.J. Watt, but it's their blend of youth-infused talent that showcases a well structured football team.

For the Eagles, I'm excited to see Joe Milton once again with what looks to be a WR core at full strength at time of writing. With extra time to prepare via the bye, I expect Milton to make some throws this week allowing his guys to make a few plays downfield. Pittsburgh is underwhelming in the secondary, and if Milton is granted just a few ticks of time to work through his progressions, the Eagles could create some explosive plays this week. Everything, however, starts with the offensive line, and I hope this coaching staff gives Markel Bell help against Watt or the longtime Steeler could have a handful of sacks by himself this weekend. That simply cannot happen. Double teams, chips, sliding protection, do something to help out your rookie RT whose confidence looks to have wavered mightily since the Jacksonville and Carolina games.

Defensively, limiting RB Jaylen Warren is the key to this week. He's the most underrated player in football in my book and the explosive engine that gets this whole thing going on offense for Pittsburgh. Limit him, and you have more than just a shot at winning this ballgame. My eyes are also on the edge rushers to create pressure on the soon-to-be 43 year old Rodgers, and the LB core which could be without Zack Baun. Shedding blocks with consistency and tackling in open space has lacked within this defense — that must improve on Sunday. Overall, I haven't seen this team adjust in months and this Steelers team is a rugged ballclub that just got embarrassed on Sunday Night football against the Bengals. Do I hope HC Nick Sirianni and OC Sean Mannion made adjustments during the bye week? Absolutely, but, my confidence in executing said changes is a whole other conversation.

#2857. Knicks Bulletin: ‘Sometimes, it just happens’

The Knicks face the Ja-less Blazers today.

New York will host this and four more games before the Knicks pack their bags again.

Here are a few quotes for you sickos sticking to the screen even on a sunny Saturday (as if I wasn't one).

On experimenting with Jose Alvarado alongside Jalen Brunson to give opposing defenses a different look:

"Just trying to give defenses a different look. Getting Jalen off the ball some, because if you think about it, Jose played with Jalen. Tyler was the backup, he was on the floor when Jalen nor Jose was on. Jose was on the floor with Jalen, and it allowed us still to match up and move Jalen around a little bit."

On why playing Alvarado alongside Brunson has reduced Miles McBride's minutes:

"The reality of it is, if Deuce plays and we still try to do that, then Jose is not playing and Jalen is back on the ball. We'll keep trying to mix it up to see what works best for us as we go forward. It could be that or it could be Jose, because it's been two games now I think that we did it, we did it that way a little bit in Toronto, too. We felt it worked well for us."

On the Knicks finishing their road trip 3-1 despite struggling to close out the Timberwolves:

"It's a win. Three and one on the road trip. So we'll take it."

On the Knicks finding their rhythm and improving their defensive execution:

"I feel like we're getting into a good rhythm offensively, defensively. I think you'll see some of the defensive rotations were on point. Obviously, we can be better. But we're continuing to learn, gain experience. Every year is different, every team is different. So I think we're doing that, and I think we're getting better. Whenever you win in the league, you continue to build confidence."

On the Knicks proving they can win games in different ways:

"It shows that we're able to win in different ways. And that's what you have to do in the league. Some days, you're gonna shoot the cover off the ball and you can win by 10, 15, 20. Other games, it might be a possession game and offensively it's not really clicking, not knocking down shots, but you're getting stops defensively and you're able to pull out stops like that. I think it shows that this team can win games in different fashions. And that's the most important thing in the league."

On the Knicks rebuilding confidence by winning close games in different ways:

"For us to have a great road trip like this and win in so many different ways, it [says] a lot about our team. It's great when you can find a way to win. At the end of the day, it's hard to win in the NBA. I've learned that. It's hard to win. And you just have to find a way to win every single night. For us on this road trip to continue to find ways to win, it speaks to this team, and it's the most important thing when you play."

On staying ready despite an unpredictable role in the Knicks' guard rotation:

"When the time comes, you can't really explain the situation sometimes. Sometimes, it just happens. When your number is called you just always gotta be ready, so that's what the NBA has been for me. I'm just glad to be in a situation where I could at least get an opportunity or at least make my voice heard and my game played."

On why Marlon Humphrey's criticism of Baltimore felt personal to him:

"I've been sitting with this one for a couple of days, because Baltimore isn't a talking point to me. It's my blood."

On witnessing the impact of crime and systemic problems while growing up in Baltimore:

"I saw what this city can take from you. I lost friends to the streets, and I watched people I loved get swallowed by a system that was built to swallow them. I know the reputation. I've heard every joke and every headline my whole life."

On accepting Humphrey's apology but demanding more meaningful action:

"Marlon, I see the apology. I'll take it, man to man. But Baltimore has been apologized to its whole life. Apologies don't build anything. Presence does… You don't get to rep Baltimore on Sundays and talk about it like an outsider on Wednesday. Baltimore doesn't make criminals."

#2856. Warren Buffett quadruples Thanksgiving gifts: A breakdown of how much wealth he's giving away

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Warren Buffett, 96, more than quadrupled his Thanksgiving donations to four family foundations this year to $5.5 billion.

The gifts consist of Berkshire Class B shares that have been converted from his roughly $160 billion of Class A shares.

The increase is in line with his new goal, announced last July, to give away all of his Berkshire Hathaway shares "within about eight years," with no additional annual donations going to the Gates Foundation.

Around $2.2 billion of shares goes to the Susan Thompson Buffett Foundation, named for his late first wife, with the three foundations run by his children, Susie Buffett's Sherwood Foundation, the Howard G. Buffett Foundation, and Peter Buffett's NoVo Foundation each getting $1.1 billion each.

In July of last year, Buffett gave shares worth $4.6 billion to the Gates Foundation with another $1.4 billion going to the family foundations.

This July, the family foundations received $5.9 billion in stock, bringing the year's total to $11.4 billion, an increase of almost 55% from last year, even as the Gates Foundation received nothing.

In comments included in the Berkshire news release this week announcing the new gifts, Buffett reaffirmed his confidence in his children's ability to disburse his fortune over time, saying they "have given thoughtfully to worthy causes for decades, and I trust 100% both their abilities and their fidelity."

He also said his confidence in Berkshire is "as strong as ever."

Recalling that in last year's lengthier Thanksgiving message he had said, "Kindness is costless but also priceless," Buffett wrote, " A year later, I would only add that it compounds, and at a better rate than anything Charlie [Munger] and I ever bought."

"Both of us received beyond measurement costless benefits from a few teachers such as Ben Graham, Joe Rosenfield and Tom Murphy. Because of this we not only prospered mightily but were able to pass along many of the benefits we received.

"Much of my good fortune has come in the form of people who were kind to me and expected nothing in return. I'm grateful to every one of them.

"This Thanksgiving, thank one of those selfless people in your own life. Better yet, be a benefactor for someone else."

Berkshire Hathaway's fourth quarter rally that had produced a string of record highs cooled this week as both classes of its stock posted five straight daily declines.

Last Friday, Berkshire was up 19.1% since October 1. Now that gain has been cut to 14.5%

The S&P's decline of 8.6% this week, however, was even bigger than Berkshire's 5-day drop, as investors sold to lock in profits from AI-related stocks.

The benchmark index, along with the Dow Industrials, are in a correction after falling 10% from their recent highs, and the Nasdaq Composite has erased all of its year-to-date gains.

Berkshire's smaller decline increased its year-to-date outperformance of the S&P to 12.5 percentage points.

One Wall Street firm, however, thinks Berkshire's run-up has run its course.

Argus Research downgraded the B shares to hold from buy Friday, saying the stock is "fully valued" at about 25 times next year's estimate for operating earnings per share.

As foreshadowed by its 10-Q financial report released almost two weeks ago, Berkshire Hathaway's Q3 13F portfolio snapshot filed with the SEC on Monday showed it wasn't doing much buying and selling of equities this summer.

And the two largest additions, Lennar and Bending Spoons, had been previously disclosed, along with additional purchases at the start of the fourth quarter.

The most notable change we didn't expect was a 12% increase in Berkshire's Delta Air Lines position, bringing it just short of a 10% stake, now valued at $5.2 billion.

Exceeding 10% triggers a number of regulatory burdens, including the requirement to promptly disclose new buying or selling, so we may not see additional purchases going forward.

Delta's return to the portfolio after Warren Buffett sold all of Berkshire's airline stocks in 2020 has been something of a surprise given his bumpy ride with the industry over the years.

As expected, Berkshire continued to trim its Bank of America stake. After nine consecutive quarters of sales, it's been cut by 56% but remains the fifth largest portfolio holding at $21.4 billion.

Berkshire also cut another financial, Capital One, by almost 62%, although it was already a relatively small position, now worth $207 million.

Also notable are two positions that didn't change.

After adding $17 billion in Q2 to the Alphabet bet that had only been initiated last summer, Berkshire defied some expectations by keeping the holding unchanged.

Its 106 million shares are valued at $28.1 billion.

And for the fourth consecutive quarter, Berkshire's largest equity holding, Apple, didn't change. With a big boost from the stock's year-to-date gain of 26.1%, the position is now valued at more than $78 billion.

Warren Buffett loves to eat what many would call junk food and drink a lot of sugary beverages. A quarter-century ago, when he was 70, Buffett explained why he still thought a life insurance company would rate his life expectancy "considerably better than the average."

AUDIENCE MEMBER: Every time I hear what you like to eat, Warren, it makes me wonder what your cholesterol level is — (laughter) — or if you even worry about it...

WARREN BUFFETT: I do know the number, and I don't remember it. My doctor tells me, "It's a little high," but if he says it's a little high, it means it isn't that high, or he would — because he always tries to push me into making a few changes in my life.

But he — I've got a wonderful doctor. And I was lucky last year, because I hadn't been in to see him for about five years. And — (laughter) — due to — those guys cost a lot of money, I mean. (Laughter)

And due to purely an accident, a reaction to some other medicine I was given when I was out of the city, he got a hold of me, and then he shamed me into having a physical. And it was extremely lucky, because I had a polyp in the colon that would have probably caused trouble, you know, within a couple of years.

I would say that if you ask my doctor, he would want me to make a few changes, but he would also say that my life expectancy is probably a lot better than the average person of 70.

You know, I have no stress whatsoever. Zero. You know, I mean, I get to do what I love to do every day. You know, and I'm surrounded by people that are terrific.

So that problem in life just doesn't exist for me. You know, and I don't smoke or drink or, well, we'll end it right there. (Laughter)

And so, you know, if you were an underwriter for a life company, you would rate me considerably better than the average. You'd rate Charlie better than average, too.

And I'm sure that, you know, I could change it slightly, perhaps, on the probabilities, you know, if I change my diet dramatically or something. But it's very unlikely to happen.

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,210,976,168,580

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

Holdings are as of September 30, 2026, as reported in Berkshire Hathaway's 13F filing on November 16, 2026, except for:

The full list of holdings and current market values is available from CNBC.com's Berkshire Hathaway Portfolio Tracker.

Also, Buffett's annual letters to shareholders are highly recommended reading. There are collected here on Berkshire's website.

-- Alex Crippen, Editor, Warren Buffett Watch

#2855. Steelers vs Eagles betting guide: Odds, props and picks

Steelers vs Eagles betting guide: Odds, props and picks originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Steelers (6-3) vs Eagles (2-7), Sunday 1:00pm

Point spread/ML: Steelers -4.5/-225 Eagles +4.5/+188

Analysis: The Steelers have had a strong season, coming in at 6-3. They had won four straight before being embarrassed on national television by the Bengals, 38-18. The Eagles are no strangers to embarrassment this season, but for the first time in several weeks, the injury report isn't looking like a CVS receipt. WR DeVonta Smith practiced in full Thursday, as did RB Saquon Barkley, and edge rushers Jonathan Greenard and Jalyx Hunt.

HC Mike McCarthy is in his first season coaching Pittsburgh, but has plenty of experience coaching against the Eagles from his days in Green Bay and Dallas. He is 3-5 lifetime in games at the Linc. Nick Sirianni is 5-0 after a bye week, but I'm not sure any amount of time will give his team enough of an advantage.

Aaron Rodgers: 211.5 passing yards, 1.5 TD (+140/-188)

Joe Milton: 187.5 passing yards, 0.5 TD (-205/+152)

Analysis: Milton did his best with the hand he was dealt two weeks ago against the Giants (18/34/169), considering he spent much of the afternoon running for his life. He'll get help with Smith – and very possibly Tay Wicks – returning to the lineup. He should also get a boost from a Steelers pass D ranked 30th in the league.

Rodgers has had an up-and-down season; he had a four-week run where he looked bulletproof, and followed it up with two games with subpar numbers: a blowout win over Cleveland, where they didn't need much from him, and their blowout loss to the Bengals, where they didn't get much.

Picks: Milton OVER yards, Rodgers OVER yards

Analysis: I feel a lot of the game script could be determined by which Jaylen Warren shows up. He has had five games with 93+ yards this season, and four with 50 or fewer, the most recent last week vs Cincy (7/15). Dowdle had three straight games of 40+ yards before last week's 5/26.

I like Milton to make more plays with his legs, as he did vs NYG (9/64). It may be a better strategy than running Barkley between the tackles. Barkley may finally be healthy, but he can't block for himself.

Picks: Barkley UNDER yards, Milton OVER yards

DeVonta Smith – 48.5 yards, 4.5 receptions

Dallas Goedert – 30.5 yards, 3.5 receptions

Germie Bernard – 29.5 yards, 3.5 receptions

Pat Freiermuth – 20.5 yards, 2.5 receptions

Darnell Washington 20.5 yards, 2.5 receptions

Analysis: One would think Smith will want to put on a show after missing 5 of the last 6 games, and a change in QB from Tanner McKee to Milton shouldn't put a wrinkle in that. Goedert has caught 4+ passes in L5.

I can't explain what happened to DK Metcalf. 35 catches in nine games is far less than I expected, and I think Steelers fans would agree. Five catches on 28 targets over the last 3 weeks is shocking. Meanwhile, Bernard had a four-game run of 25 catches before being shutout last week. Warren has 3 catches in 6/9 this season, and 26+ yards in 8/9, he's Rodgers' main checkdown option.

Picks: Smith OVER yards, OVER receptions; Warren OVER receptions, OVER yards

Analysis: Either Steelers TE has scored in six straight. Play them both, just not in a parlay.

Picks: Barkley, Milton, Washington, Freiermuth

#2854. Ford scores twice as Stanford controls Cal 44-26 to reach bowl eligibility

BERKELEY, Calif. (AP) — Micah Ford ran for two touchdowns while throwing for one and rushed for 151 yards in Stanford's 44-26 win over California in the Big Game on Saturday.

The junior running back scored twice early in the third quarter to help Stanford (6-5, 4-4 in Atlantic Coast Conference) pull away.

Davis Warren went 14 for 25 for 214 yards and two touchdowns.

Stanford is bowl eligible for the first time since 2018.

The Golden Bears (3-8, 2-6) opted for sophomore quarterback Jaron-Keawe Sagapolutele late in the second quarter. He finished 14 for 21 with 127 yards and a touchdown.

He replaced Dominic Ingrassia who played most of the first half mostly handing off. Jackson Brousseau threw a late touchdown.

Stanford led 7-0 on a 1-yard pass from Ford to tight end Chico Holt. The 13-play, 54-yard drive stayed alive with the help of a pass interference call.

Cal responded with an 11-play, 75-yard drive culminating on Taimane Pucell's 2-yard rushing touchdown. The Bears never threw on the drive.

Warren rushed for an 8-yard touchdown with 5:38 left in the second to put Stanford ahead 14-7.

Sagapolutele threw a 12-yard touchdown pass to Ian Strong to tie it again with 45 seconds before half. Stanford went to the half up 17-14 on a 14-yard field goal following a 52-yard fumble return from Darrius Davis.

Ford scored on a 1-yard plunge early in the third. The two-point conversion failed and the lead was 23-14. Minutes later he broke free for 63 yards to account for his third touchdown. Warren found Nico Brown for 49 yards in the opening minutes of the fourth to put Stanford up 37-14.

Adam Mohammed punched in a one-yard score for the Bears' first second-half touchdown since Oct. 17. The Cardinal closed their scoring with a 48-yard Zach Giuliano touchdown reception.

Stanford: The Cardinal took the Big Game for the second straight year after having lost the previous four. It was Ford's fourth game with at least 149 yards rushing over his last five.

California: The Golden Bears have been outscored 110-14 over the past four second halves. They have been outscored 211-72 over the five-game losing streak, the program's worst run since 2022.

California: Hosts Pittsburgh on Saturday.
