#2830. Is Dillon Brooks the Suns’ most valuable trade asset?

Stabilization. Now that we're 15 games into the season, we're starting to see some of that happen for the Phoenix Suns. They sit at 8-7, albeit as the 9th seed in the Western Conference, with plenty of basketball still to be played. We're beginning to see the vision Brian Gregory had this past offseason manifest itself on the court on a nightly basis.

And we're starting to see fewer people running to the trade machine in an attempt to fix the Phoenix Suns.

Naturally, that's part of being a fan. We recognize deficiencies and attempt to find solutions, however wild or unlikely those solutions might be. Given the tools at our disposal these days (I heart you Fanspo), it's become increasingly easy to explore potential trades. We all do it, and it's part of the fun of following a team. It's also part of the frustration, because many of the proposed solutions are completely illogical. We blindly assume another organization would gladly accept whatever trade proposal we've concocted within the confines of a trade machine.

The same could be said about a recent proposal from Fadeaway World's Siddhant Gupta. In his latest column, he made three suggestions that would send Dillon Brooks to the San Antonio Spurs. One was for Tobias Harris, with the addition of two first-round picks. The second was to send Keldon Johnson and a first-round pick to Phoenix for the Villain. The third? Exchange Brooks for Harrison Barnes, Luke Kornet, and a protected first-round pick.

What was your initial reaction to that one? Disgust? An immediate, "I'm not gonna do it"? My reaction was one I didn't expect to have. It had less to do with the proposed trade itself and more to do with the player involved. It raised a simple question: Is Dillon Brooks the Phoenix Suns' most desirable asset?

Before I dive into this whole thought exercise, I want to make one thing clear right off the bat. I have no desire for the Phoenix Suns to trade Dillon Brooks at this point in time.

He's the heartbeat of this team, the culture setter. And while he can be an inefficient shooter at times and make some poorly timed decisions, his presence is ultimately a net positive that extends well beyond the stat sheet. He helps define who this team is, and when we get an engaged version of Dillon Brooks, he lifts everyone around him. He brings out the energy and identity that make this team competitive and an absolute annoyance for the opposition.

For all those reasons, especially when you factor in his $21 million salary this season, Brooks becomes a highly desirable asset.

Look at Devin Booker. His contract is simply too much for most teams to absorb. At $57.1 million, acquiring him would require quite the haul from an opposing organization. Truth be told, as we've experienced here in Phoenix, having Devin Booker as your Batman doesn't necessarily mean you're going to take over Gotham.

Jalen Green? We've been so focused on different trade scenarios and potential returns that we've perhaps overlooked the reality of his value. Despite his elite athleticism and potential upside at 24 years old, he hasn't demonstrated that he's worth the $36.3 million he's being paid this season.

But Brooks? If you're an organization looking for a culture setter, someone who brings toughness and establishes an identity, Dillon Brooks can provide exactly that. And he does so on a contract that makes him an incredibly desirable asset.

Let's expand on this thought exercise a little further and take a longer view of what a potential Dillon Brooks trade could mean for the Phoenix Suns, without factoring in anything they might receive in return, shall we?

Brooks signed a three-year, $73 million extension this past offseason, keeping him on the books through the 2029-30 season, when he'll be making $26.1 million. As we know, the salary cap will continue to rise over the next three seasons, potentially by as much as 10% annually, with each increase compounding on the previous one. A more realistic projection is somewhere around 6%. Using that figure, the salary cap would be roughly $185 million in the final year of Brooks' contract.

The Suns are currently projected to have $145.3 million committed to salaries that season, which also happens to be the final year of Devin Booker's extension. That leaves Phoenix with approximately $40 million in cap space and five players under contract. Booker, Brooks, Collin Gillespie, Koa Peat, and Oso Ighodaro.

There's one significant factor missing from the graphic above, however. A Miles Bridges extension. Because, like it or not, it's coming. You don't surrender an unprotected 2033 first-round pick to acquire a player you ultimately have no intention of bringing back. Given Bridges' production thus far this season, I believe there's enough to justify an extension, albeit at what I would project to be a manageable number based on how this front office has operated recently.

So let's add a sixth player to the equation and hypothetically assign Bridges a $30 million salary for the 2029-30 season. There you have it. Six players under contract and approximately $10 million in cap space.

Brooks will be 33 entering that season, which by no means suggests he'll have one foot in the grave as a basketball player. However, aging players tend to become less effective and less desirable when you factor in the money they're making relative to their production, along with the increased injury risk that comes with age.

When you look at this from a bigger-picture perspective, knowing that Brooks and Bridges are somewhat duplicative in what they provide on the court, you can begin to talk yourself into the idea that the Suns' most desirable asset could help address some of their immediate deficiencies, most notably size, while also providing long-term relief on the salary cap sheet. It would create financial flexibility, open opportunities that have yet to present themselves, and make those future salary commitments easier to manage.

If you trade Brooks now for someone with only one or two years remaining on their contract, you've potentially cleared $26 million from the books in 2029-30. That gives Phoenix some financial flexibility as it prepares to enter the final season before Bradley Beal's dead money finally disappears. (Dear Lord, it still won't be off the books by then?! Nope. Not until the summer of 2031!)

In short, you're cleaning up the books in preparation for a time when the Suns will finally have money available to make some moves. You're positioning yourself to strike when the right opportunity presents itself.

Who could be available in free agency that summer? Luka Doncic. Anthony Edwards. Mikal Bridges. Pretty much anyone drafted in 2025 who hasn't signed an extension with their respective team.

Look at a team like the Utah Jazz, who the Phoenix Suns played last night. That's a team with an abundance of size. They were without Jusuf Nurkic, Jaren Jackson Jr., Lauri Markkanen, and Kyle Filipowski, yet still had Jaxson Hayes and Mo Bamba providing quality minutes as big men who could rebound and be an annoyance on the glass. I'm not suggesting you ship Dillon Brooks off to Utah, but that's certainly a team that could use a culture setter right now. They looked like a collection of individuals rather than a cohesive unit, although what else can you expect from a team dealing with that many injuries?

But who knows? Who knows where the Phoenix Suns will be on February 8? After all, that's when Brooks becomes trade-eligible after signing his extension this past offseason, only three days before the February 11 trade deadline.

At that time, they'll be closer to getting Mark Williams back, certainly, but how much do we realistically expect him to provide in terms of rim protection when he returns? He'll be coming off a significant injury and will likely be on a minutes restriction as the organization eases him back into the rotation. They recently committed to him with an extension, and the last thing they want is to risk any long-term consequences to his health.

So as we continue playing with our trade machines, and while disappointment may exist with the production of Jalen Green, I believe Dillon Brooks is the Suns' most desirable asset.

Again, I have no desire to trade him at this point. There's plenty of basketball left to be played, and we still have a lot to learn about this team. But if you're a fan looking for ways to improve the Phoenix Suns, putting Dillon Brooks into the trade machine might be the most logical place to start. His contract, production, and presence make it much easier to understand why another organization would be interested in acquiring him. Because every team that plays the Phoenix Suns finds Dillon Brooks annoying as hell. Every one of those teams would love to have a player like him. He's the kind of guy you despise when he's wearing the opposing jersey and absolutely love when he's wearing yours.

I love Dillon Brooks about as much as I love cheesy cauliflower on Thanksgiving. And if you've never had it, you're missing out. Make it with Cheez Whiz. It's delicious. Of course, you should also know that it's one of those dishes that'll have you farting for a week. Which, come to think of it, is pretty much how I felt about that proposed trade sending Dillon Brooks to the San Antonio Spurs. Ferrrrrrrt.

#2829. Colts release starting QB Daniel Jones less than a year after extending him

Colts release starting QB Daniel Jones less than a year after extending him originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Daniel Jones played so well in his first 10 games with the Indianapolis Colts that he entered the MVP conversation.

His last 11 games were so bad, he lost his job.

On Friday, less than 24 hours after yet another abysmal offensive performance in a 26-3 loss at Houston, Colts coach Shane Steichen said the team was releasing Jones and turning to Anthony Richardson as the starter when it faces the New York Giants next week. It's the second time in three years Jones has been released in November, the first coming after he sought and received his release from the Giants in 2024.

Jones then joined the Minnesota Vikings practice squad before signing a one-year deal with the Colts in March 2025 and a two-year deal worth $88 million earlier this year. This time, though, Indy — not Jones — made the call.

"This was a decision that was made for, you know, the best interests of both parties," Steichen said, explaining Jones had not asked for his release. "It's just where we're at in our season. That's just the situation we're at right now."

For the Colts (3-8), it's another key move in what already has been a tumultuous season.

Team co-owner Carlie Irsay-Gordon fired general manager Chris Ballard last month, and Indy then traded four key players — All-Pro guard Quenton Nelson, Pro Bowl defensive tackle DeForest Buckner, Pro Bowl receiver Keenan Allen and receiver Josh Downs — before this month's trading deadline. Nelson and Buckner were captains and longtime locker room mainstays while Allen and Downs were Indy's top two receivers.

Now they're cutting ties with Jones, who was going to be a $46.8 million salary cap hit next season. Instead, the Colts will save about $11.6 million, as they give Richardson, the No. 4 overall pick in the 2023 draft, a second chance to prove himself. Steichen said he had spoken with Irsay-Gordon before announcing the move but that he made the final decision.

"I think there's a lot of factors, I don't think it was just on one person," Steichen said. "Obviously, the quarterback takes a lot of the blame, obviously, that's the business we're in. But things, you know, just didn't work out."

Jones started his Colts career fast, leading them to an 8-2 mark on one of the most productive offenses in league history through 10 games. But Jones wasn't the same after breaking a bone in his left leg last November and struggled mightily this season after returning from last December's season-ending torn right Achilles tendon.

His completion percentage, 58.4%, and passer rating, 65.4, rank among the lowest of all NFL starters. He also struggled with turnovers, throwing 13 interceptions and losing three fumbles. The increasingly poor performances had fans calling for a change.

Jones followed his season-worst passer rating of 41.5 in Sunday's 24-22 victory over the one-win Miami Dolphins with his second worst single-game rating this year, 45.9, on Thursday night. He was 12 of 27 for 144 yards with one interception and was sacked for a safety.

Still, Steichen fully supported Jones, insisting he gave Indy the best chance to win.

"I can't say enough good things about Daniel, just the ultimate competitor, ultimate teammate," Steichen said. "The toughness he brought to our football team — I mean the guy played on a broken leg for us last year for three or four games, snapped his Achilles, came back 8 1/2 months later and gave this organization everything he had. I couldn't be more thankful for the time I had with him."

Richardson replaced Jones in the fourth quarter Thursday. He was 0 for 2 with one carry for 2 yards.

Now, the guy who has twice requested to be traded since losing the quarterback battle to Jones during training camp in 2025, will take over. The former Florida star has struggled with accuracy, posting a career completion percentage of just 50.6% while throwing 11 TD passes and 13 interceptions.

Richardson also has battled injuries, playing in just 15 of the Colts' 34 games when he was the starter. This year, he's 2 of 4 for 8 yards in three games.

But now the guy who was once billed as the future face of the franchise will get a new opportunity. Second-year quarterback Riley Leonard is the only other quarterback on Indy's active roster.

"I talked to Anthony today, I told him, obviously, he'll be the starter versus the Giants," Steichen said. "There's, obviously, a world in which Riley could play as well so we'll evaluate that each and every week throughout the season."

#2827. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

#2826. Lakers coach’s halftime message paid off vs Kings: ‘He lit me up’

With his team lacking the requisite toughness and energy during the first half of Friday's matchup against the Kings, Lakers coach JJ Redick delivered a different kind of halftime message.

And he did so in a way he hadn't done that much during the team's first 16 regular-season games, which they won 13 of.

Los Angeles Lakers coach JJ Redick had a passionate message to deliver to his team during halftime. AP Photo/Caroline Brehman

"I feel I did a decent job of regulating at halftime," Redick said. "They may have a differing opinion of that. Can't repeat every word I used. The main message was, 'We've got to be tougher, we've got to bring a little bit of energy.'"

The level of intensity that Redick brought to the locker room was nothing new for Luka Dončić after having played for Redick over 1 ½ years.

"I mean he yells, but I'm used to it," Dončić said.

But the Slovenian superstar felt that Redick's methods were necessary after the Lakers trailed by eight points to a one-win Kings team that had lost its previous seven games.

"Got us all fired up," Dončić said. "Sometimes you get to yell."

The Lakers responded to Redick's message, winning the second half 52-42 to beat the Kings 106-104 and avoid losing consecutive games for the first time this season.

The team's starting center, Walker Kessler, was among the players who took Redick's message to heart.

Kessler, who recorded a points-rebounds double-double in the previous six games and 10 of the previous 11, had just one rebound and four fouls to go with six points at halftime.

"He lit me up a little bit, rightfully so," Kessler said. "But it was constructive. He was like, 'I know you can do this. Do it.'"

Walker Kessler responded well to JJ Redick's halftime message. Getty Images

Kessler had team highs of nine points and six rebounds after halftime.

His biggest blemish in the final two quarters was missing a pair of late free throws that would've haunted the 7-foot-1 big man and the Lakers had Zach LaVine's buzzer-beating 3-point attempt converted.

But he also had one of the biggest highlights of the second half when he made his first 3-pointer of the season, a pull-up from the left corner to give the Lakers a one-point lead early in the fourth quarter.

"I wasn't playing [physically]," Kessler said of Redick's message to him. "I wasn't going up to dunk the ball. I was missing some more touches around the rim. And then defensively, being undisciplined. Jumping at stuff I don't need to jump at. He definitely lit into me. It was good. I needed it. That's what makes a good coach. Knowing when guys need that and when guys don't."

And it was clear the Lakers not only needed the message Redick delivered but also appreciated how it was delivered after not playing their best basketball over the last week to kick off the four-game homestand that ends Monday against the Jazz.

"At the core of everything that all of our coaches say, and JJ especially, they just speak to the truth of what's happening on the court," Matisse Thybulle said. "And if it needs to be said passionately, that's the message. That's how he needs to get it across, and that's what he does.

"But ultimately, JJ doesn't really mince his words, and he doesn't play games. He really gets to the point, and I think as players, we all really appreciate that. And like he understands, and we understand where he's coming from because he's been in our shoes as well."

#2825. Lincoln Kienholz throws 5 TD passes and No. 21 Louisville forces 4 turnovers in 42-10 win over Pitt

LOUISVILLE, Ky. (AP) — Lincoln Kienholz threw a season-high five touchdown passes and Louisville's defense forced four turnovers Saturday night as the 21st-ranked Cardinals rolled to a 42-10 victory over Pittsburgh.

Facing the nation's second-stingiest rushing defense, the Cardinals (8-3, 7-2 Atlantic Coast Conference, No. 23 CFP) needed their quarterback's arm to earn their sixth straight win.

Kienholz completed 25 of 41 passes for 309 yards, with three of his five scoring throws coming in the second half. After halftime, he completed all nine passes for 149 yards.

Mistakes led to all the first-half points for both teams. Louisville forced three Pitt turnovers, with two fumble recoveries in Panthers territory leading to touchdown tosses by Kienholz.

The Panthers (6-5, 3-5) managed just 89 yards in the first two quarters, but the defense forced Kienholz to fumble. Isaiah Neal scooped up the ball and scampered 31 yards for a tying score with 9:22 left in the half.

Louisville was driving in the final minute of the second quarter when Alex Sanford picked off Kienholz at the Pittsburgh 29 and returned it 43 yards with 51 seconds remaining. A holding penalty and a sack cost Pitt 11 yards, but Antonio Chadha still made a 57-yard field goal as time expired to cut the deficit to 14-10.

The kick was a yard short of the school record, which Chadha shares with two others.

Louisville had 160 yards of offense in the first half but netted zero on the ground thanks to three Panthers sacks. The Cardinals finished with 396 yards, including 87 rushing.

Pitt travels to Cal for its regular-season finale next Saturday.

Louisville ends its regular season with a visit to No. 17 Kentucky on Saturday.

#2823. Future Eagles Fits: Five interior defensive linemen to watch in 2027 NFL draft

Future Eagles Fits: Five interior defensive linemen to watch in 2027 NFL draft originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Welcome back to Future Eagles Fits, our weekly deep dive into the prospects who could eventually find their way to Philadelphia.

Every week, we're going inside the tape and looking at prospects through an Eagles-centric lens — breaking down the athletic profile, positional versatility and scheme fit that could ultimately determine whether a player makes sense in Philly. Along the way, we'll examine how prospects are being evaluated across the league as draft boards begin to take shape and conversations are had with league representatives across the scouting landscape.

For the Eagles, the interior of their defensive line has quickly become a position to monitor as the organization begins to look ahead. A first and second round pick in return for Jordan Davis is outstanding additional draft capital to work with, but it also leaves a significant piece of the defensive front to replace moving forward. Jalen Carter remains the centerpiece of the group, while Moro Ojomo, Ty Robinson, and Byron Young will have opportunities to take on larger roles within the rotation. Still, adding another young, explosive presence to the interior could go a long way toward maintaining the physicality and disruptive ability that has become such an important part of the Eagles' defensive identity.

The good news? It's an intriguing class up front with several players who offer unique skill sets that could make sense for what the Eagles are looking to add. Whether it's finding another high-end interior pass rusher, adding a versatile defensive lineman who can align up and down the front, or bringing in a bigger-bodied presence who can control the line of scrimmage, there are legitimate options to consider.

While there is still a ton of football left to be played and the entirety of the pre-draft process still to play out — which will alter boards — here are five interior defensive linemen to know in the upcoming class — two potential Day 1 targets, two Day 2 prospects and one Day 3 option who could provide some additional juice up front.

Day 1: Xavier Gilliam, Tennessee | 6'2″, 307 lbs (verified measurements)

If you're looking for an interior presence who can immediately change the way an opposing offense has to approach its protection schemes, it starts with Gilliam in this class.

A former transfer from Penn State, Gilliam has developed into one of the most explosive defensive players in the entire country this season and he's currently a top-10 player on my personal board. Below is a quick peek at the type of pop he provides up front.

At nearly 310 pounds, Gilliam has an outstanding combination of lower-body explosiveness, strength and initial quickness off the snap. He doesn't need much space to generate momentum, and his ability to explode out of his stance allows him to create immediate problems for guards attempting to establish angles.

There's a level of violence to his game that really stands out, as well. He plays with outstanding energy, consistently attacks the line of scrimmage and has the type of first-step quickness that can make even well-positioned offensive linemen uncomfortable. He's not simply a defensive tackle who occupies space and looks to disrupt the run game, he has legitimate pass-rush juice and the athletic profile to create negative plays without needing a blitz or additional help from the second level.

For Philly, he and Carter would be a true nightmare to counter due to their explosiveness up front. Add in Ojomo – we'll see if they do in fact extend him – and all three would give the Eagles an outstanding foundation along the line of scrimmage. If you're looking to invest first-round capital in an interior defensive lineman, finding someone who can make an impact in both phases of the game is a pretty good place to start. He's one of the most exciting defensive prospects in the entire class.

Day 1: Devan Thompkins, Alabama | 6'5″, 290 lbs (verified measurements)

Gilliam is the explosive interior disruptor, but Thompkins might be the most versatile defensive lineman in the entire draft.

A transfer from USC to Alabama, Thompkins has the opportunity to be the first interior/edge hybrid selected next April. At 290 pounds, he brings a unique physical profile to the position, and I'm not sure there's another player in this class who offers quite the same flexibility when it comes to moving around a defensive front.

The biggest selling point with Thompkins is that he doesn't necessarily need to be confined to one particular alignment. He can line up directly across from a guard and attack the interior, work outside an offensive tackle, or even operate as a wide-nine defender outside the tight end. It's an incredibly valuable skill set for any defensive coordinator who wants to manufacture different looks, create favorable matchups and keep opposing offensive lines guessing.

There's real power behind his game, too, which matters for young players making the transition to the pro game. It can't all be finesse, and Thompkins has herculean strength to generate movement at the point of attack, and his ability to attack from different alignments makes him particularly difficult to counter from down to down. He has the athleticism to challenge offensive tackles on the outside, but also possesses the strength and power to create problems when working inside against guards.

What makes his evaluation particularly interesting is the number of different ways an NFL coaching staff could utilize him. Want to kick him inside and create a mismatch against a guard? Sure. Line him up outside the tackle and let him use his length and athleticism to get after the quarterback? That's another option. He's the type of player who gives a defensive coordinator the freedom to be creative with personnel and alignment without sacrificing physicality, and for the Eagles, that versatility could be especially appealing. Philadelphia has traditionally valued defensive linemen who can move around the front and create problems from multiple alignments. With Carter and Ojomo already providing interior options, Thompkins could give the Eagles another player who allows them to change their defensive presentation depending on the opponent.

Day 2: A.J. Holmes Jr, Texas Tech | 6'2″, 300 lbs, 32 ⅜ arm (verified measurements)

Holmes is a name whose draft stock has continued to trend upward throughout the process, and there's a lot to like about what he brings to the interior defensive line.

At 300 pounds, he has a compact, powerful frame that allows him to hold his ground against offensive linemen while also providing pass-rush upside.

He's capable of winning against individual blocks with his power using his upper-body strength to create separation and disrupt the timing of opposing run concepts, but he's not a player who needs to rely exclusively on athleticism to make an impact. There's a physical element to his game that allows him to compete at the point of attack and create problems for offensive linemen who aren't prepared for his initial punch.

He's also shown the ability to provide something as a pass rusher, which is particularly important when evaluating interior defensive linemen in this range. Holmes has enough quickness to attack gaps and create pressure from the inside, giving him a pathway to contribute on passing downs rather than simply being a rotational run defender. His ability to generate disruption without requiring a favorable matchup is an important part of his evaluation, and he's excelled so far this year totaling 11 sacks through 10 games for the Red Raiders.

There are still areas to develop, particularly when it comes to consistently anchoring against combination blocks and maintaining his positioning when offenses devote additional attention to him, but that's part of the appeal of finding a player here on Day 2.

For Philly, Holmes would provide another young body who can compete for snaps in the middle of the defensive line. Established pieces are present, but continuing to add depth and competition to the rotation is important, particularly after moving Davis. Holmes has the ability to contribute against the run while offering pass-rush upside, making him an intriguing developmental option who could eventually grow into a larger role.

Day 2: Jordan Hall, Georgia | 6'4″, 320 lbs, 35 ¼ arm (verified measurements)

The Bulldogs have consistently produced NFL-caliber defensive linemen, and Hall is another player whose combination of size, length, and physicality makes him an intriguing prospect in the class.

The bottom line for the Georgia product – he's an immovable force in the ground game.

There's a level of natural strength to his game that makes it extremely difficult for offensive linemen to create movement against him. He has the length to control a rep early on, size to occupy space, the lower-body strength to anchor against double teams and the physicality to make life difficult for opposing offensive lines attempting to establish their running game. He's not necessarily going to win every snap by making a spectacular play in the backfield, but his ability to screw his cleats into the grass and prevent offenses from creating movement at the point of attack is what makes him so appealing.

And sometimes, that's exactly what a defense needs.

There's also a natural fit when considering the Eagles' recent investment in Georgia defenders. Roseman has consistently shown an appreciation for the program's defensive talent, and Hall would give the Eagles another opportunity to bring in a player from a pipeline they know well.

After moving Davis, adding another big-bodied presence who can control the middle of the defensive line would make plenty of sense. Carter and Ojomo offer different skill sets, and Hall could complement those players by providing a more traditional interior run defender who can eat up space and make it difficult for opposing offenses to establish their identity on the ground.

Day 3: Jide Abasiri, USC | 6'4″, 281 lbs (verified measurements)

I hope Abasiri ends up at an All-Star game in the pre-draft process for another evaluation exposure as he could end up being one of the more intriguing value selections in the middle to late stages of the draft.

A young prospect who just turned 21 years old, Abasiri has all the physical tools NFL teams drool over when going through the evaluation process. At 6-foot-4 and 285 pounds, he brings excellent length to the interior defensive line and has shown enough flashes throughout the season to suggest there's a lot more potential to unlock as he continues developing.

The first thing that stands out is his physical profile, as Abasiri has the type of length that can create immediate problems for opposing offensive linemen, particularly when he's able to extend and establish his hands to keep blockers from getting into his frame. For a younger player, that combination of size and length is an appealing starting point, especially when considering how much development can take place within ideal coaching surroundings in the NFL.

At this stage of the process, consistency is going to be an important part of his evaluation. There's a difference between flashing the ability to win at the college level and consistently executing those techniques against NFL-caliber offensive linemen. Abasiri still has room to develop his overall game, particularly when it comes to translating those physical tools into more consistent production, and it's a main reason why he's in this range to begin with.

Right now, he's someone who could land anywhere from the late third round into the early-mid portion of Day 3, depending on how the rest of the evaluation process unfolds. His age, physical makeup and flashes of disruption give him an appealing developmental profile, and a strong finish to the season followed by a good showing throughout the pre-draft process will absolutely help his stock continue to climb.

For the Eagles, they don't necessarily need every defensive lineman it drafts to come in and immediately command a significant snap share. Finding younger players with legitimate physical upside who can develop behind established veterans is an important part of maintaining a productive defensive front over time, and Abasiri has the length, athleticism and developmental runway to make him an intriguing option later in the draft.

#2820. Montverde Madness in Michigan, as Scottie and ‘Em take down the Pistons

The Raptors needed a win last night, like a person dying of thirst needs a beverage. As the adage goes, you can lead a horse to water, but you can't make them drink; and if you're Scottie Barnes, you can lead the Raptors to a win, even if you're playing without RJ Barrett and Collin Murray-Boyles.

In a game that came down to the wire, Scottie Barnes took over in clutch time, taking the ball to the bucket and dropping it in to put the Raptors up 103-102 over the team that's currently third in the Eastern Conference. A Cade Cunningham pass that was picked off by Allen Graves got the Raptors a crucial possession that resulted in them getting two free throws from Immanuel Quickley.

This forced a three from Detroit to stay alive, giving Scottie Barnes the game-winning contest over Kevin Huerter, letting Toronto seal the game, 105-102. While the first win against the Chicago Bulls came easier and let the Raptors make their mark on the NBA, this win was a life vest for Raptors fans, showing the masses what this team can do, even when down numerous crucial pieces.

The drafting of Collin-Murray Boyles and Allen Graves in back to back years felt like asking for clones of Scottie Barnes on the Raptors, and with Boyles sitting out tonight's game on concussion protocol, the rookie had his chance to put his stamp on the game, and that, he did. The rookie was the team's second leading scorer after Scottie, posting 20 points and 10 boards, career highs in both, as he stepped into the starting role. Deadly from three, the forward hit two treys for the Raptors.

Indeed, Cunningham and Barnes were teammates and classmates in high school, part of the legendary 2019-2020 Montverde Academy basketball roster. "Cade and 'Em" have formed a crux of high school basketball discourse, with endless debate about a hypothetical matchup between them and the 2015-16 Chino Hills team, led by the Ball brothers Lonzo, LiAngelo, and LaMelo.

But, with Scottie Barnes ascending in the league, he's making Montverde proud, even if it's at the expense of perhaps their favourite son.

Barnes himself had 29 points, 5 assists, and 5 rebounds, a balanced statline that demonstrates yet again Scottie's development being far from a straight line. Holding down the fort without many of his usual primary or auxiliary scorers, Scottie gave the Raptors their first win in seven games, in what may be a signature victory for the struggling team. This may be the Raptors' most impressive win of the season, and may be the turning point for a team to rally around the ascent of Scottie and Allen Graves, riding that rocket high into the Eastern Conference standings.

#2819. Jaguars saw something in Liam Coen that other teams didn't

In a season and a half with the Jaguars, Liam Coen has established himself as one of the best coaches in the NFL. Last year, Coen took over a team that had gone 4-13 the year before and improved their record to 13-4 — the best record ever for a first-year coach taking over a team that had won four or fewer games the year before. This year Coen is showing that last year was no fluke, with the Jaguars at 7-2 with an NFL-best point differential of +125.

Coen’s great success should have other teams that hired new head coaches in 2025 asking themselves: What did we miss? Because the Jaguars were so high on Coen that he was their No. 1 target and they were willing to do whatever it took to get him, while no other team even bothered to give Coen an interview.

During the 2025 NFL coaching search, we tracked dozens of reports of head coaching interviews and requests among the seven teams that had vacancies. The Jaguars were the only team that so much as asked Coen to interview.

This week there’s been some media scrutiny on the Jets, who hired Aaron Glenn without considering Coen as a candidate. Glenn is 5-22; Coen is 20-6. But it’s unfair to single out the Jets for failing to interview Coen, because no team other than the Jaguars did, either.

The 41-year-old Coen had a résumé that should have made him a good candidate. NFL teams are always on the lookout for coaches who can nurture a franchise quarterback and run an innovative offense. Coen played quarterback at UMass and coached quarterbacks in college before getting hired by Sean McVay to work on the Rams’ staff, eventually became the Rams’ offensive coordinator under McVay, and had been a successful playcaller as offensive coordinator for the Buccaneers, as well as at Kentucky. With that background, it’s surprising that only the Jaguars wanted him.

And the Jaguars really wanted him, to the point that they were willing to do whatever it took to hire him. Coen initially turned the Jaguars down and agreed to a contract extension to stay with the Buccaneers, but Jaguars owner Shad Khan wouldn’t take no for an answer. When Coen said he was concerned about the direction of the Jaguars’ front office, Khan fired General Manager Trent Baalke and agreed to reshape the front office in a way that Coen would approve of if Coen would take the job. Coen was persuaded to take the Jaguars job, and the Jaguars later hired James Gladstone, who had worked with Coen on the Rams, as GM.

The result has been the Jaguars going from one of the worst teams in the NFL to one of the best. Coen’s success in Jacksonville has justified the faith Khan had in him. And raises questions about the total lack of interest from the rest of the league.

#2818. ‘What's different? My confidence': Joe Milton used bye week to master offense

'What's different? My confidence': Joe Milton used bye week to master offense originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The bye week gave Joe Milton a chance to do stuff he really had to do.

Milton made his first NFL start after just 23 days and 10 practices with the Eagles and it went about as expected in a 19-11 loss to the Giants, the Eagles' seventh straight loss.

Two weeks later, Milton feels like a different man.

Thanks to the bye week, he's had two weeks to devote to learning an offense that he just didn't have time to learn before his first start.

It's still not a lot of time, but 37 days is a lot different than 23 days.

"What's different for me now? My confidence," Milton said after the Eagles returned to practice Wednesday. "Just confidence in knowing this offense and what these coaches and my teammates around me are instilling in me and talking to me about.

"And just my reason to ask some questions. I'm great at asking questions, and I ask Parks (Frazier) and Sean (Mannion) a lot of questions. And I don't think I can ask (more) questions than I already do. I think I do a great job at that. So just yeah, just my confidence for sure.

"I feel more comfortable than I did last week for sure. Just the verbiage and everything that we needed to do … to solve the problem and fix it."

Milton wasn't awful in the Giants loss considering the circumstances. He didn't have DeVonta Smith or Dontayvion Wicks and the offensive line was unable to protect him, on top of his lack of reps in Mannion's offense.

He'll make his second career start Sunday at the Linc against the Steelers, who are 6-3 but ranked 30th in pass defense, worse even than the Eagles.

For the record, the last Eagles quarterback to lose his first two career starts was Nick Foles in 2012.

For Milton, the bye week couldn't have come at a better time.

"It was very helpful just because I was able to communicate (with) the coaches (about) what we need to do better and what we need to get better at and where I need to be better and just echoing that and fixing the protections for myself. Learning it, studying it more, being able to pick up the blitzes whenever they show up, and just really just understanding the verbiage that we're pronouncing and saying here."

Milton completed 18 for 34 passes for 169 yards against the Giants with a long gainer of 20 yards, no touchdowns or interceptions. He also ran nine times for 64 yards and ran for a two-point conversion. The Eagles are last in the NFL in total offense and 30th in passing offense and scoring.

One thing Milton did during the bye was ask tons of questions. That's big for him, a good way for him to learn. He's big on questions.

"I ask questions every day," he said. "It could be the same question. I just want to make sure I heard correctly and I took down the right note. I'm big on writing notes. And my coaches do a great job of just like overly talking, just constantly mentioning the same thing. And I mean, you kind of hear it every day and all throughout the week, so it kind of gets stuck in your brain."

"Just what do I need to do on this play?" he said. "Like, what is what is the quarterback job specifically on this play? That goes for every play. Like, what do you exactly want me to do on this play? And if there's anything different, just tell me now."

Milton will have Smith and Wicks back Sunday, so that should help.

"Those two guys coming back obviously helps out the team," he said. "For me to throw to those guys today was amazing. I mean, shoot, I couldn't stop smiling for real if you ask me."

The Eagles haven't won a game in two months so there hasn't been a lot of smiling going on around the team facility.

A more settled, more prepared Milton could go a long way toward ending this ugly seven-game losing streak.

"I know what I need to know as far as this offense and whatever goes on in this offense," he said. "So I think I'm in the right place and the right time as far as where I'm at with this offense.

"It's not rocket science. Football is football, no matter where you go. It's all about X's and O's and just knowing football. Coverage is going to be the coverage, a play is going to be a play, and if it's a run game, just hand it off to the running back."

#2817. JJ Redick hopeful Austin Reaves will return in next two games

While the Lakers have been fairly calm about Austin Reaves' heel bruise injury, the team has also now gone over a week without getting him back on the court.

Head coach JJ Redick has stated there is no structural damage and it's a matter of pain tolerance, which is encouraging long-term. However, it must be a particularly painful injury because, while Reaves has gotten on the court in recent days, he still has, obviously, yet to return for a game.

Ahead of Friday's contest against the Kings, Redick provided an encouraging update on Reaves and a potential timeline for a return.

#2814. Eagles-Steelers matchups to watch in Week 11

Eagles-Steelers matchups to watch in Week 11 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles (2-7) are hosting the Pittsburgh Steelers (6-3) at the Linc on Sunday afternoon at 1.

The biggest move Howie Roseman made before the trade deadline was sending Jordan Davis to the Jacksonville Jaguars for a first-rounder and a second-rounder. So this will be the Eagles' first game without their run-stuffing nose tackle and they're facing a team that is averaging 4.4 yards per carry on the ground — the fourth-highest average in the NFL.

The Steelers are 14th in overall rushing but have been efficient this season. Their ground game starts with running back Jaylen Warren, who has 126 carries for 719 yards and 2 touchdowns this season. That 5.7 yards-per-carry average is the best in the NFL among qualified players. Of Warren's 719 yards this season, 533 of them have come after contact. He's averaging 4.2 yards after contact per attempt, which is the fourth-highest average in the NFL. So it'll be important for the Eagles to wrap up in this game.

The good news for the Eagles is that they had their best run defense game of the season before the bye week against the Giants, allowing just 80 yards on the ground. It was the first time all season they held their opponent under 100 rushing yards.

Eagles LBs and S vs. Pat Freiermuth, Darnell Washington

The Steelers' leading receiver this season is DK Metcalf, who has 432 yards and 2 touchdowns on 35 catches. He is also leading the team in targets with 70. So maybe this is another good week to have Quinyon Mitchell travel with a top receiver. But the Steelers also have two tight ends who each see a ton of action in Pat Freiermuth and Darnell Washington.

Freiermuth: 38 targets, 27 catches, 270 yards, 5 TDs

Washington: 31 targets, 20 catches, 259 yards, 3 TDs

The Steelers have 15 passing touchdowns this season and 8 of them come from these two players. So the Eagles have to be aware of them in the red zone.

The Eagles have actually been very good against tight ends this season. They have allowed 38 catches for 386 yards and 3 touchdowns to opposing tight ends this season. Just two teams (Titans and Jaguars) have allowed fewer yards to opposing tight ends. But this will be a big test for their safeties and linebackers, especially if Zack Baun (back) is unable to play.

Aaron Rodgers is one of the greatest QBs in NFL history but he's obviously past his prime. Rodgers will turn 43 on Dec. 2, but he's having an OK season. He has completed 59.9% of his passes for 2,014 yards with 15 touchdowns and 6 interceptions. But he has already been sacked 26 times in nine games. The only QBs who have been sacked more this year are Deshaun Watson (37), Justin Herbert (33), Geno Smith (29) and Bryce Young (29).

Despite that, the Steelers are allowing a pressure rate of just 31.4%, which is the seventh-best QB pressure rate in the NFL. The 49ers are tops at 27.9%. While Rodgers is getting sacked quite a bit, he's also getting the ball out quickly. His average time to throw is 2.75 seconds, which is eighth in the NFL.

The Steelers are going to be without starting left guard Mason McCormick, who suffered an ankle injury in the loss to the Bengals.

The Eagles' defense finally broke out before the bye week with six sacks against the Giants after having just 10 entering the game. But with 16 sacks, they still rank 28th in the NFL this season and their pressure rate of 32.6% ranks 26th.

The plan this season was to have third-round pick Markel Bell sit and learn behind all-time great Lane Johnson but that plan got thrown into a blender earlier this year when Johnson abruptly retired. While Bell has had some rough moments, it'll be his job for the rest of the season. That became abundantly clear when the Eagles released Fred Johnson late last week. In six games, Bell has given up 5 sacks and 19 pressures. There have been ups and downs as the Miami product adjusts to the NFL.

This won't be an easy week. T.J. Watt leads the Steelers with 7 1/2 sacks and is second in pressures on the team with 23. (Don't sleep on Alex Highsmith or Nick Herbig; this is a dangerous group of edge rushers.) Watt, 32, is a future Hall of Famer and a former Defensive Player of the Year who is fourth on the active sack list with 122.5. He's behind just Von Miller (139.5), Cameron Jordan (132) and Myles Garrett (128.5). Oh yeah, and he plays most of his snaps on the defensive left, which sets up this matchup against Bell. The Eagles will need to help the rookie in this game.

Before the bye week, Saquon Barkley was dealing with hamstring and back injuries that were limiting him. Barkley looked good in limited action against the Cowboys but the last two games had 26 carries for 52 yards. Barkley has looked good when healthy this season but he's also running behind an offensive line that has really struggled this season. He'll see a tough test in Week 11.

The Steelers this season have the NFL's No. 3-ranked run defense. They have given up just 83.2 yards per game on the ground in 2026 and have held opponents to 43.3 rushing yards per game over their last three games. They have given up just 0.71 yards before contact per attempt.

DeVonta Smith vs. Asante Samuel Jr., Jamel Dean

In the Steelers' Week 10 loss to the Bengals, they had no answer for Jamar Chase, who ended up with 11 catches on 14 targets for 231 yards. Based PFF's data, Chase caught a pass against six different Steelers defenders but did most of his damage against their top two corners:

Chase vs. Samuel Jr.: 4 targets, 2 caches, 96 yards

Chase vs. Dean: 5 targets, 4 catches, 53 yards

The Steelers have been playing Jalen Ramsey at nickel corner and safety. He's no longer a full-time outside cornerback, so we won't see him on Smith in this game. At one point, the Steelers had a cornerback who would have had a better shot against Smith but they were unable to reach a contract agreement with Joey Porter Jr. and traded him to the Cowboys in late September.

Smith has missed the last three games with a hamstring injury but if he's healthy in this one, it could be a big opportunity for him in his first game with starting QB Joe Milton.

#2813. Rob Pelinka on Lakers: 'We’ll be active at the trade deadline'

The Los Angeles Lakers sit on top of the Western Conference at 14-3, with the third-best offense in the NBA — behind an MVP-level start to the season from Luka Doncic — and a solid defense. Walker Kessler has been exactly what the Lakers hoped for as a big in the paint, and their moves for role players that better fit around Doncic — Matisse Thybulle, Sandro Mamukelashvili, Quentin Grimes, Quentin Grimes — have largely worked out.

Lakers general manager Rob Pelinka has seen it, too, and thinks this team has a deep playoff run in them. Which is why he said in an interview on the Lakers pregame show that the team would be "active" at the trade deadline (hat tip Real GM).

"We'll be active at the trade deadline to shore up things that we see. We think this team has a real chance to make a run, so we want to give the group as much talent as we can. Don't want to get too far over our skis with a hot start, because you don't want to get big-headed, but clearly the way it's gone so far, it's been great, and we hope it continues."

Active is a broad term. The Lakers will be active talking to teams, whether they make a deal will depend on the players available, and the price.

The Lakers' most critical need is to improve at backup center behind Kessler. Kevon Looney has largely fallen out of the rotation, leaving the Lakers playing small ball with Sandro Mamukelashvili or Jarred Vanderbilt at the five, or leaning into two-way big Carson Cooper. There may be better options available.

The Lakers don't have draft picks of note to trade, but they have players, and while they are hard-capped at the first apron, they are a little more than $8 million below that line and have room to work.

United have won one, drawn five and lost four of their 10 league matches, with 1-1 draws against Fulham, Tottenham Hotspur and Leeds United before the 4-4 draw with Villa in which they twice came from two goals down. Eight of their last nine permanent or caretaker managers avoided defeat in their first game, Erik ten Hag the exception.

#2810. Nvidia has lost $2 trillion in value. Here's what options traders expect from earnings

Jensen Huang's forced exit from Nvidia shocked Wall Street and Washington, wiped $2 trillion off its market value, and raised serious questions about national security and regulation of the AI complex. Traders, for the moment, must content themselves with the comparatively mundane business of Tuesday's earnings, and options traders are expecting big moves.

With volatility in the stock near the highest since April 2025, options traders are now pricing a 7% reaction in the stock after its report, the biggest implied move going into an Nvidia report since February 2025, and well above the median move of 3.2% over the last eight quarters.

Implied volatility reached 68 Monday, compared to under 40 in early October, according to ThinkOrSwim data. Nvidia vol reached a multi-year high above 92 in the aftermath of President Trump's tariff Liberation day in April '25. In other words, the company that for much of the AI boom was the most reliable winner in the market, is now more volatile than all but 35 companies in the S&P 500.

"Longer term, I see significant downside risk unless Nvidia can attract an exceptional leader, someone like Tim Cook or AMD's Lisa Su," Nigam Arora, founder of the Arora report, said in a text ahead of the company's after-market report on Tuesday.

Since hitting all-time highs in October, Nvidia shares have fallen 35%, shedding $2 trillion in market capitalization along the way. Including Nvidia, there are only five companies in the S&P 500 worth more than $2 trillion. However, there is evidence that bulls have been holding out hope while bears snatch up profits: the ratio of open puts to call contracts has actually widened towards calls, according to Barchart data. The ratio is currently 0.87, down from a one-year high of 1.02 two weeks ago and the lowest since the first week of October.

The most popular contract by volume on Monday was the 155-strike put expiring at the bell Monday, but the most popular trade for later expiry was the 160-strike call expiring Thursday, and the second-most-common purchase was the 190-strike call expiring Friday, SpotGamma data show.

One catch is that most call contracts traded Monday looked like neutral to bearish positions opened by traders selling calls. Of the 5 million options traded on Monday, 3 million were calls, but more were likely opened by sellers than buyers. Of the $2.3 billion in options premium traded on Nvidia Monday, $1.2 billion was tied to puts, SpotGamma data show.

With bears taking profits in puts the past month, there's another potential snag on open interest. There are very few open put contracts at or below the $157 level at which the stock closed Monday. That suggests any bad surprise would find many traders with little downside protection.

By the same token, there's notable open interest in call contracts from the 175-strike and above, suggesting profit-taking on a bounce could limit Nvidia's recovery past levels it breached over the past week.

Either way, Tuesday's earnings could provide fireworks.

#2809. Bayern Munich News: Nicolò Tresoldi on FC Bayern’s radar?; Arsenal, Liverpool, Chelsea, and Manchester City eyeing Jamal Musiala; and MORE!

The best-laid plans of mice and men often go awry.

Bayern Munich had (and maybe still has) zero plans to enter the transfer fray in January. However, big injuries to several key players — including Harry Kane — might push the Bavarians to rethink that strategy. If so, an Italian outlet is reporting that FC Bayern could make a play for Club Brugge striker Nicolò Tresoldi:

Milan now faces another (very formidable) competitor for Nicolò Tresoldi: Bayern Munich. The Germans are already keeping tabs on the Bruges forward, but Harry Kane's injury last night could prompt Kompany's team to act quickly in order to replace the Ballon d'Or winner with a forward of great potential as early as the beginning of January. Someone like Tresoldi, in fact.

The Club Brugge striker is highly regarded in Bavaria. In recent weeks, he was considered a long-term investment, perhaps to be finalized in 2027-28. Now, however, the situation has changed and an immediate reinforcement is needed. Bayern have the money to acquire him in the January transfer window, and the allure they could exert on the player is considerable. At least as attractive as AC Milan, Nicolò's favorite team since childhood. Therefore, Kane's injury is not good news for the Reds and risks significantly complicating the race for one of the Rossoneri's transfer targets. Club Brugge, who already turned down offers between €35 and €40 million in August, value him at least €60 million.

Tresoldi has been on Bayern Munich's radar for a while, but the club has not really had an impetus to push the issue. Now, there could be a reason.

As exciting as that move might be, we are still a little wary of thinking the Bavarians will shell out that much in January. What do you think?

Paris Saint-Germain star Désiré Doué went down clutching his knee and might have suffered a torn ACL:

Concern after the euphoria. This Saturday, on the pitch of the Allianz Riviera, Désiré Doué scored before suffering what appeared to be a serious knee injury. Having recently injured a tooth while on international duty with France, the Parisian striker had nevertheless started for PSG in this match following the international break.

While the 21-year-old's jaw seems to be holding up well, as he celebrated his goal by showing off his mouthguard , there is cause for concern regarding his knee. Just minutes after putting his team back in front (2-1, 32nd minute), he collapsed before colliding with Diop, screaming in pain and clutching his knee (40th minute). Footage shows his knee joint giving way completely. He ultimately had to be stretchered off and replaced by Ferran Torres.

According to initial reports from the club, there is serious concern. PSG suspects a torn cruciate ligament. An MRI scan is scheduled for Sunday.

All of a sudden, there is at least a little doubt about Jamal Musiala's future at Bayern Munich.

As you would expect, that has created some excitement for a few Premier League clubs — namely Arsenal, Liverpool, Chelsea, and Manchester City:

Premier League clubs are expected to be on red alert amid fears at Bayern Munich that Jamal Musiala's wages are no longer feasible. The 23-year-old was diagnosed with a fainting condition in the summer after collapsing on the pitch twice in just a few days.

According to talkSPORT, Liverpool are 'on red alert' as Musiala has a release clause that will allow him to leave Bayern next summer. Sky Germany journalist Florian Plettenberg revealed the exit clause on Thursday. It is rumoured to be worth €175million (£147m).

talkSPORT write that Liverpool and Arsenal hold 'long-term interest' in Musiala, while Chelsea and Manchester City also admire him. Bayern chief Karl-Heinz Rummenigge has suggested the club might look to sell the 23-year-old as he is on huge wages.

Given Musiala's struggles in recent years, Bayern may be forced to consider selling him for well below that £147m release clause. The key for interested clubs such as Liverpool and Arsenal would be whether they can keep Musiala healthy and fit.

When he is playing regularly and in full flow, Musiala is a world-class operator who can unlock opposition defences. But Liverpool and Arsenal will not want to pay big money for him if they cannot get any guarantees over his long-term fitness.

As of now, it seems like Musiala is content to see this rough patch out at Bayern Munich. However, you could argue that if Bayern Munich is not able to work out a pay cut with the Germany international, it might be open to selling him (just ask Karl-Heinz Rummenigge).

It seems like this might be a little…too early to start thinking about this kind of move, but given how things have escalated, who knows where this all leads?

Sport Bild journalist Christian Falk has information that contradicts reports out of Spain, which indicate that Real Madrid might be giving up on its dream pursuit of Bayern Munich star Michael Olise:

❌It is NOT TRUE: Real Madrid are backing away from Michael Olise after Bayern set a €300m [£251.4m] price tag. It's complete nonsense! Bayern have categorically not decided to set a price tag of €300m for Olise. I love my Spanish colleagues, but I'm afraid the reports coming out of Spain on this particular matter are inaccurate. I was also reading about another claim that suggested FC Bayern had already made a contract offer to the French international, and he said no. No such conversation has been had!

On Monday, Bayern held a supervisory board meeting where they discussed a few topics. Olise was not a main topic. The board are firm on the idea that the club won't sell the winger, so Real Madrid and other interested parties can do whatever they want. Bayern will open talks with him, but not right now this minute. Next year for sure! However, they feel comfortable with the idea of waiting until the summer to formally open talks. It has to be emphasised that Michael Olise has three years left on his deal. If they're going to sort out a new contract, it makes a certain amount of financial sense to wait until the end of the season anyway, and then they can save some funds to put toward his new contract. If they wait until the summer, they can give Olise three additional years on his current terms (keeping in line with the five-year limit in Germany).

They're waiting for the best moment. Bayern are aware of interest in the player. They know that Premier League clubs like Arsenal would love a player like Olise. On that point, Bayern would refer any interested suitor to club president Herbert Hainer's comments (reiterating Karl-Heinz Rummenigge's original statement): "We are not a selling club, but a buying club." Bayern Munich have the self-confidence to stick to this philosophy.

This week, we looked into the salaries of the Bayern squad in our magazine, Sport Bild, and Harry Kane sits at the top of the pile at around €29m [£24.3m] gross, according to our information. You can see that there's quite a big gap between the €14m [£11.7m] Michael Olise is earning and Kane's €29m. Bayern Munich have quite a few arguments in their favour to get the French winger to put pen to paper, but let's see what happens! Olise has all the options in the world to take his pick of any elite club in world football. But Bayern Munich will fight to keep him in the Bundesliga.

Every club has Michael Olise on their shortlist. There's a lot of respect for Arsenal, but there are some really big players at the table, like Real Madrid. Olise has already played in the Premier League, so I strongly suspect a move to La Liga would be the next logical step were he to look for a move away from Munich. That all said, Bayern Munich have proven that big personal awards like the Ballon d'Or can still be one at FC Bayern. So, that's the hope of the club and they'll do everything in their power to keep Olise at the Allianz Arena.

AC Milan and Galatasaray are both reportedly interested in Manchester United attacking midfielder Bruno Fernandes:

AC Milan and Galatasaray have shown an interest in signing Manchester United attacking midfielder Bruno Fernandes as a free agent in July, as reported by Nicolo Schira. United have offered the 32-year-old a contract extension which would keep him at Old Trafford until 2029, but there is currently no agreement between the two parties. Fernandes turned down a large offer from Saudi Arabian side Al Hilal last June and is reportedly waiting for to see how Pochettino turns out before he commits himself.

#2808. Arnold Ebiketie: No hard feelings after trade to Bengals falls through

Arnold Ebiketie: No hard feelings after trade to Bengals falls through originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Arnold Ebiketie was at breakfast last Tuesday when he got the call.

The Eagles were trading him to the Bengals for a 2027 sixth-round draft pick. As Ebiketie drove home to pack his bags, his mind was racing. He was already thinking about how he was going to ship his car to Cincinnati, pack up his belongings and spend some time with his kids before heading to Ohio. His flight had already been booked.

But about two hours later, Ebiketie got another call. The deal fell through.

"It's the NFL, the business-side of it," Ebiketie said on Wednesday as the Eagles began their preparations to play the Steelers. "I felt like one call I was going to Cincinnati, another call I was staying here. I wasn't mad at the fact that I was staying so I got back to my bye week. That was the best part about it."

The Bengals were looking for some added edge rusher depth before the deadline when they agreed to the trade with the Eagles, but they somehow overlooked the fact that trading for Ebiketie would cancel out a projected third-round compensatory pick coming their way in next year's draft. Ebiketie might have been worth a sixth-round pick, but he wasn't worth losing a third-rounder too.

So the Bengals had to embarrassingly call off the deal and the Eagles had to welcome back a player they very obviously tried to trade.

You would understand if Ebiketie found the situation a little awkward, but he claims it isn't.

"Nah. A lot of people ask me but I wouldn't say it makes me feel awkward," he said. "It's football. Now I'm back at work, I've got to get ready to play Pittsburgh this weekend. It's been normal."

Ebiketie said the first thing he did when he learned about the trade was to look up the Bengals' schedule. He was hoping that Cincinnati hadn't yet had their bye week and was disappointed to learn they already had it in Week 6.

Had the trade gone through, Ebiketie would have had to forfeit a bye week and join the Bengals immediately.

"Getting traded on your bye week, that's not the best thing to happen," Ebiketie said. "But then I realized I was staying, I was like, 'You know what? I can get back to my plans,' which I wasn't doing much. Going to DC for the weekend. Got back to my plans."

The Eagles signed the 27-year-old Ebiketie to a one-year deal this offseason and he has played in all nine games this season. Ebiketie has played a total of 300 defensive snaps (48%) but has yet to register a sack and has just two quarterback hits.

After four years with the Falcons, Ebiketie had to take a one-year, prove-it deal in Philly and he hasn't been very successful. The team hasn't been very successful either.

But Ebiketie is happy to still be in Philly. He's especially happy to have more time with his teammates.

"That's the hardest part, you know," Ebiketie said. "When you make the connections with the guys and you get traded and you have to leave and that's the first thing that goes through your head. The people that you're around in the locker room, people that you're out there playing with, you're definitely going to miss them."

→ Baca artikel asli

#2807. Brad Stevens talks Mitchell Robinson, Celtics’ start, and more

SACRAMENTO — Brad Stevens took a lot of heat this offseason for pulling the trigger on the trade that sent Jaylen Brown to the Philadelphia 76ers and ended a near-decade-long partnership with Jayson Tatum.

And, though it's too soon to tell, the Celtics are 11-4, have the second-best net rating in the NBA, the second-best defense, and the sixth-best offense. Paul George, the team's biggest offseason acqusition, has been generally awesome. The team's significant depth has been on full display regularly.

Still, Stevens said it's too soon to judge who this team really is, and that he normally waits until mid-December to make those kinds of evaluations.

"I've been encouraged by a lot of things," Stevens said. "I think we've still got a lot to figure out to be the best version of ourselves. But usually, I'm probably another couple of weeks or a month away from getting too deep into, okay, this is who we really are. Yet, I think we have played some good basketball. We've shown ourselves capable of it. We've gotten good play from a lot of different guys, and the team has really tried to buy in, I think, well, to what the coaches are asking them to do on both sides of the ball."

Paul George has been one of the brightest spots of the Celtics' early-season success. George has been tremendous defensively, and he's also averaging 19.9 points, 5.5 rebounds, and 3.7 assists per game, while shooting 46.5% from the field and 42.3% from three. He's had two games with at least 35 points and has been one of the team's most reliable defenders.

"Paul's been a good fit," Stevens said. "Paul's a really good player…. You don't come with those accolades if you're not able to impact the game. Everybody talks about what he can do on offense, and I think the minute he stepped on the court with our guys defensively, our guys, a lot of them were kind of taken aback, even though playing against him all these years, with how good he is, how active he is, how smart he is, how aware he is, and he's just really had a good impact on the group. He's just been a joy to be around. He's been great."

The Celtics' other marquee offseason acquisition, Mitchell Robinson, hasn't transitioned as seamlessly as George. Robinson has the worst on-off rating on the Celtics at -30.1 (that number measures the difference in net rating when a player is on the floor versus off of it). The historically prolific rebounder is also averaging the fewest rebounds per game (5.4) of his career.

"You can tell it's obviously a transition for him, and that's not all that atypical for guys, and so again, it's so early that we'll see how this all plays out," Stevens said. "But I trust that what he does when he's at his best will translate because it always has, right? And I think that getting him to the rim on both sides of the floor, getting him to be in that area where he can be most impactful, [is] something that we'll continue to work on — continue to put him in position where, hopefully, he can soar with his strengths."

Then, there's rookie Chris Cenac Jr., who began the year with the Maine Celtics but has found himself in the Celtics' rotation over the past two games. Cenac, who was drafted 27th overall, tallied 6 points and 6 rebounds in 17 minutes in the Celtics' loss to the Nuggets on Wednesday night.

"He's been solid," Stevens said. "I'm really encouraged. I really like him. I think he's got a great work ethic. He's obviously got physical tools. He's got a good mind for the game. He's gonna be good, right? I do think that with each step, there are different expectations. So he's in that honeymoon phase of, well, he did one good thing and it looks awesome, and wow. And then your expectations grow and grow, and it's just like we've seen with Jordan and Hugo and Baylor. Once you taste it, once you get a little feeling, then the expectation is to be even better, and we see that all the way up and down our roster. All of our guys, for the most part, have been through that. But I'm encouraged by the start. It's hard not to be, and I think the other part of it is he does have the physical ability to continue to raise his level in big moments."

One of the other interesting aspects of the roster is the logjam at the wing position; Sam Hauser (20.3 minutes), Baylor Scheierman (19.4 minutes), Jordan Walsh (15.7 minutes), and Hugo Gonzalez (12.9 minutes) have all seen significant minutes, but no one player in that group has pulled away to play the majority of the minutes.

Each brings something different to the table; Hauser (46.8% from three) and Scheierman (42.2% from three) have been the biggest threats from beyond the arc, while Gonzalez (7.1 rebounds/36 minutes) has been the best rebounder of the bunch. From the wings, Walsh has probably been the team's best on-ball defender and its most consistent starter, with 10 starts in 15 games.

But the players have seemed interchangeable at times, and no one has clearly emerged as the best player in the pack.

"I said at the start of the year, the biggest challenge for the coaching staff is the wing differentiation," Stevens said. "That's been the hardest part, and I think they've managed it beautifully, because nobody has really differentiated themselves, and they're all good players. And so that's just kind of the hard part about having depth, which in this league you need now. It's really important, but it also comes with the challenges of — not everybody's going to get exactly what they want on every given night. Is it easier to have seven or eight guys that you know are going to play every night? Yeah, until two of them get hurt, then you're in trouble, right? And so that's part of the challenge in this day and age with how the league is going and how hard it is to play the full 82, and certainly game to game with what's required on both ends."

As the team continues to improve on both ends, Stevens is generally encouraged by what he's seen.

"One of the things that this group really has is a lot of feel for how to play, and you see that when we're at our very best on both ends: the reaction, the reads, the ability to play off one another, the ability to cover for one another on the defensive end," Stevens said. "I think that those are things that, as the season goes on, we should continue to benefit from, to be able to grow, kind of however Joe and the staff want us to play throughout that given stretch, and ultimately to be the best that we can be."

#2806. Jerry Jones on fan's death after fight at 49ers-Cowboys game: "We had no record of an incident"

This week, a fan died after falling during a fight at AT&T Stadium. The fan was there to watch the 49ers-Cowboys game.

On Friday, Cowboys owner and G.M. Jerry Jones was asked about the incident during an appearance on 105.3 The Fan.

“I just got that information, and so we had no record of of an incident, and we’ll be obviously looking into that,” Jones said. “My understanding is that the individual went to the game and didn’t feel good at the game, and then left, and [had] been hospitalized. But we weren’t aware of any incident that involved a fan at the game.”

They know about it now, and they have the resources to fully investigate.

“We really do have quite a — eons above what it was 10, 15 years ago,” Jones said. “We have quite a surveillance of what’s going on at every aspect of our game. And so it is really rare if we didn’t know of any incident. We’ve got too many people out there. We have 6,000 workers on game day.”

Jones said he’ll pass along any surveillance footage the Cowboys have to investigators.

A full and complete investigation could potentially create liability for the venue. It all depends on the facts.
