#2805. Eagles notebook: Nolan Smith reacts to the Jordan Davis trade

Eagles notebook: Nolan Smith reacts to the Jordan Davis trade originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Nolan Smith and Jordan Davis were teammates at Georgia and then became teammates again in the NFL. They have been friends for a long time.

"Man, he's a great guy," Smith said on Friday. "That's part of the business. That's how it goes. I'm pretty sure Jacksonville is picking up a good player. But we have to focus on us. We have leaders in this locker room. We've got guys who can lead and push guys."

Davis was traded to the Jaguars ahead of the trade deadline last week and this was the first chance to catch up with Smith since the trade. So it has been over a week since it all went down. Jalen Carter, who also played with Davis at Georgia before becoming teammates in the NFL, was again not available to reporters this week.

The return on the Davis trade for the Eagles was undeniable. Getting a first-round pick and second-round pick for Davis represented incredible value. But it doesn't mean they won't miss Davis.

"Man, it was special," Smith said of their time together in Philly. "But that time come and go, man. I'm still playing with guys that I played with at Georgia. But that was a real special moment, though."

The Eagles are back from their bye week and Sean Mannion is still their offensive coordinator and play-caller. Mannion this week even said he got a vote of confidence from Nick Sirianni.

"We're in conversation all the time," Mannion said. "Nick's been totally supportive since I got here in, what was it? I guess February, January. We remain in lockstep and it's great getting to work with him every day.

"Like I said, it's the same things I'm saying about the joy I get working with the players in here. The same can be said about our coaching staff, whether it be with Nick, with our assistants, everybody across the board, it's been totally positive."

If Sirianni decided to hand over play-calling to a different coach during the bye week, it would have been understandable. After all, the Eagles are 32nd out of 32 teams in yards this season and are 30th in points. But this is Mannion's offense and going to someone else (like Josh Grizzard) likely wouldn't fix the problems.

Mannion, 34, is new at this. It's his first year as an NFL offensive coordinator and his first time as a play caller. There was always likely going to be a learning curve; but it has been steeper than many envisioned.

But the fact that Sirianni has been asked multiple times this season if he's sticking with Mannion is telling. A lot of the outside criticisms of Mannion are warranted. Mannion tries to block out that discussion.

"I don't pay attention to any of that stuff," Mannion said. "I'm just totally focused on the role I'm in and doing everything I possibly can to help our players be successful, help our team be successful. You mentioned it, but just kind of blocking out the outside noise. I'm doing everything I can every day to help the Eagles and to help us win and be a successful offense. If there's things that are kind of orbiting outside that, it doesn't pertain to that task and that focus that I have on what's going on here."

Eagles quarterback Joe Milton is known for his strong right arm, but he's not a fan of a nickname that has been attacked to him since college: Bazooka Joe.

He prefers the nickname Mr. Rocket, "because bazookas only travel so far."

With this in mind, let's take a look at some nicknames for other Eagles players in 2026. We got some help from ProFootballReference and the Eagles' media guide for this:

Saquon Barkley: SaQuads, ShayshawnZack Baun: The Bald EagleMarkel Bell: The Liberty BellCartavious "Tank" Bigsby: Tank, Bam}Jalen Carter: Baby Rhino, BreadmanBritain Covey: Crash Test CoveyLandon Dickerson: Big CountryJake Elliott: Jake the MakeTanner McKee: The Mormon MissileQuinyon Mitchell: Quinyonamo BayMoro Ojomo: Coach MoTy Robinson: Vanilla GorillaDeVonta Smith: Smitty, Slim ReaperJeremiah Trotter Jr.: Axe Man Jr.Dontayvion Wicks: Wicked, John WickRiq Woolen: RiqtheFreak

Saquon Barkley made it clear that he doesn't know exactly what T.J. Paganetti's role is going to be with the coaching staff now that Paganetti was brought back as a consultant during the bye week.

But Barkley on Friday realized he never really publicly praised Paganetti for his contributions during Barkley's 2,000-yard season. So he took the chance to do that.

"I think having T.J. back is great," Barkley said. "I don't really know to the extent of how involved he's going to be. I'll leave that up to the coaches. I will say in '24. I'm not saying what happened in '24 is going to happen (again). I'm just saying, giving him props. In '24 he probably was like the secret sauce that no one really knew or talked about. He played a key role in the way we were able to play that year and run the ball at a high level.

So it was just good to see his face and have a conversation with him."

Vic Fangio has been coaching in the NFL for decades and is known for his sometimes gruff demeanor. But even he has a soft spot for the recently traded Jordan Davis.

"Yeah, it's just part of the NFL business," Fangio said. "We're going to miss Jordan. I'm going to miss him personally. Got a little emotional with him when we talked about it after it happened.

"I've got a lot of players that over the years I've remained in touch with and had a good relationship with, some 40 years ago, 30, 20, 10 years ago. He's one of them. So, I'll miss him personally, but he'll do good and we'll be ready to roll."

The Eagles late last week waived veteran tackle Fred Johnson, who played an important role with the team for several years.

From 2023-26, Johnson played in 58 games with 14 starts. He resurrected his career in Philly, which led to this unforgettable moment in the 2024 season when Johnson had to enter the game for Lane Johnson in a 15-12 win in the Superdome.

This was Johnson's first real playing time since the 2021 season. And all of his teammates were thrilled for him.

"After that touchdown to Saquon, I almost cried on the sideline," Johnson said. "I ain't going to lie. I got emotional. I got really emotional because I thought I'd never be back in action. Everybody knows some of my story. It's just been a long time coming. When I seen him running to score, I got hype, real hype. I was just grateful for the moment. I'm ready to go back out and do it again."

Here are some streaks to keep in mind as the Eagles go into this game on Sunday:

• After winning their first two games of the season, the Eagles have lost seven straight. That's the longest active losing streak in the NFL after the Chargers were able to get a win on Monday Night Football. With a loss on Sunday, this would be the longest Eagles losing streak since they lost eight in a row in 2012.

• The Eagles have been home underdogs 10 times during Nick Sirianni's time as Eagles head coach. They are 0-10 in those games and have covered just once. The Eagles enter this one as 4.5-point underdogs against the Steelers.

• Under Sirianni, the Eagles have been very good coming off the bye week. They are 5-0 in those games. They are one of three teams to be undefeated off bye weeks since 2021, along with the Ravens (6-0) and Bills (5-0).

There's a chance this is something that interests only me. But sometimes when there aren't a ton of obvious questions for Michael Clay, I like to get into the weeds with some special teams topics. This week, I asked him about how the Eagles coach the defense in situations where they stay on the field for a punt return.

A couple of these have come up this year. One happened against the Giants in Week 9 when the defense stayed on the field for a 4th-and-1 in the first quarter. Riq Woolen — not normally in as a jammer — ended up getting called for an illegal block.

Here was Clay's answer about getting the defense ready for those situations:

"I talk about it a lot on Fridays in our meetings right here. Obviously, I have all the defense on Saturdays in our walk-through, where we're able to go through a lot of it and talk about different things. What does this coordinator like to do in fourth-and-short scenarios? Who's their personnel? Is it a running back? Can this punter throw? Just little tidbits in there so they know, hey, alert. Something very similar to last year with Detroit. We knew (Dave) Fipp and Detroit ran a lot of fakes in that 4th-and-2, 4th-and-3 area, but it's just my job to give these guys the information. Hopefully, they can retain it, and a lot of the time they do, so they keep themselves, one, from getting hurt and, two, alert for something, a team trying to steal an extra possession, especially in a defensive back-and-forth."

#2804. Sixers get lucky with their 14th win as Herro can’t get off game-tying three in time

The Sixers just outlasted the Milwaukee Bucks by a literal second, hanging on to win 132-129 Saturday afternoon, notching their fifth win in a row and improving to 14-3 on the season.

Joel Embiid did a lot to make up for a subpar shooting night, finishing with a team-high 26 points, a season-high 17 rebounds, and nine assists shooting 7-of-17 from the floor. After missing last game with a foot sprain, Tyrese Maxey had one of his quietest games of the year finishing with 17 points on 4-of-11 shooting.

LeBron James played quarterback for much of the day, finishing with 21 points, six rebounds and six assists going 9-of-14. VJ Edgecombe shot 5-of-12 from the floor to finish with 16 points with three rebounds and six assists. Tyler Herro led all scorers with 50 but his three to tie the game just didn't beat the buzzer.

Jaylen Brown and Dean Wade missed their second straight game, both dealing with hamstring strains, along with Adem Bona, who's missed the last eight. Milwaukee was without Ousmane Dieng.

The Sixers got two of their easiest buckets all season to open the game off of cuts. James went down the baseline and Edgecombe flashed to the middle of the paint. Their pull-up jump shooting was a different story early. Embiid even had a midrange blocked by Myles Turner as the Sixers struggled to capitalize on a 1-of-8 start for the Bucks.

Embiid also had a layup attempt blocked by Turner as he started the game 0-of-4 from the field. His other two jumpers falling just off the rim. On the other end the Sixers allowed two offensive rebounds to turn into second-chance points as the Bucks' offense was the first to find a rhythm.

The Sixers' offense settled in after an early timeout from Nick Nurse. They got a couple of open threes from Anfernee Simons and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Edgecombe broke Turner down off the dribble, and LeBron opened up plenty of space on a drive to hit Embiid with a lob. James made a great one-handed pass cross court to find KCP in the corner a few possessions later but the three rimmed out. KCP and Embiid also both missed fast break layups that halted a run.

Jabari Walker checked in next to Embiid at the four like he has in recent games and was rewarded for cutting by Embiid shortly after checking in. Dominick Barlow made his season debut a few minutes later, relieving Embiid. Labaron Philon Jr. remained in the rotation and picked up a floater, but also fouled Herro attempting a three right before the quarter ended. Herro made all three to give the Bucks a one-point lead after one.

Barlow's first big impact was tipping back a missed Maxey floater for Philly's first points of the second. On the next possession he ran down the floor to catch a lob after LeBron had just about singlehandedly created. It wasn't all perfect — the Bucks scored six straight field goals in the paint, but it was a fine first shift back for Barlow.

LeBron was easily the best player on the floor for the Sixers. His ability to lead people open gave them a lot of easy baskets that were much needed for an offense struggling to hit jumpers. They were also struggling to finish off defensive possessions as the Bucks picked up their 10th second-chance point on eight offensive rebounds.

Maxey got downhill quick and drew a foul which gave Nurse a chance to use a timeout. Coming out of that break, it seemed like there was an ever bigger focus to run quickly, whether if it was after a make or a miss. After Maxey zoomed through for a layup, Edgecombe got down the floor twice, setting up Walker and Embiid for buckets at the rim.

It was weird because the Sixers weren't playing great defense by any means, but the Bucks still had a bunch of airballs in the first half, more than any NBA team should. The misses the Sixers got were ugly, but they hadn't made life hard on Herro at all — he was able to get to just about every spot on the floor.

The first trouble Herro ran into all night was when he tried to contest an Embiid dunk in transition and took a shot to the face while unsuccessfully doing so. The dunk seemed to finally get Embiid going as he was able to bully past Turner for an and-1 after trying all half to do so. That capped off an 8-0 run that gave the Sixers a one-point lead at the break.

Sandwiched in between a pair of free throws from Maxey and Embiid, LeBron got to the basket for another big dunk. A few possessoins later, Embiid finally got a midrange to go over Turner. Not much had changed defensively as the Bucks scored on their first four possessions of the second half.

The Sixers definitley got more comfortable in their half court offense as the game went on. After paint buckets from Maxey and Edgecombe, KCP knocked a three to give the Sixers a lead they were able to hang on to for more than one possession. Aside from losing Herro once on the perimeter as they came down the floor, the defense had improved as well. Embiid had started to do a good job of finishing possessions as he passed his season high in rebounds.

Simons and KCP were the only Sixers who had made a three to this point in the game, so it was a good thing they were both feeling it. Simons ripped eight straight points to give the Sixers their first double-digit lead of the night.

Embiid threw a nice one-handed pass into the corner for Edgecombe who finally became the third Sixer of the night to hit a three. Edgecombe tried to run quickly off a made free throw, but had his layup attempt blocked by Kel'el Ware. Simons got a chance for yet another buzzer-beater, but his midrange attempt didn't fall, neither did Embiid's humorous attempt to get a shot off with 0.2 seconds remaining. The Sixers held a six-point lead after the third.

Barlow did get up a corner three in his first shift and airballed it pretty bad. He got another chance in his second shift and got plenty of arc to bury it this time. LeBron found him from the high-post for a layup on the next possession to prompt an early Bucks timeout.

Shortly after James found Barlow cutting to the basket again and hit him for the layup, drawing a foul in the process. The Sixers lost their grip on Herro coming out of the timeout though, letting him get to the basket a couple of times. The Sixers nearly forced a turnover, but left AJ Green open in the process, who was able to hit a three to cut it back to a one-possession game.

After calling a timeout, the Sixers put the ball in LeBron's hands, who was able to back in his way in for a bucket, putting back his own missed floater. Thanks to another offensive rebound from Barlow on the next possession, Maxey was able to scoot to the basket for two more. James bullied his way for two more baskets, drawing a foul on one, to restore order a bit.

Milwaukee was able to respond with a basket nearly every time the Sixers scored a big one, but they couldn't get stops with LeBron locked in. They ran the same play just about every time down the floor, having Maxey come up to screen for LeBron while Embiid had the ball, and it got LeBron open every time.

Herro missed an open floater down six with a minute, which seemed like it would put the Bucks away for good. Not only did Embiid come up empty on both Sixers possessions, but the Bucks scored quick off those misses, cutting it back down to two with 12 seconds left. The Sixers hurried to beat the Bucks down the floor and Turner had to foul Embiid, picking up his sixth of the game. Embiid buried both free throws, but Brayden Burries stepped back into the corner, burying the open three to cut it to one with 4.8 to go.

Embiid went way into the backcourt on the inbound to take three seconds off the clock before being fouled. It was hard to tell from press row, but he definitely stepped out of bounds running towards the sideline and got lucky the refs missed it. He made both free throws to make it a three-point game with 1.5 seconds left and the Bucks had one timeout left to advance. The Sixers put Barlow in for Embiid. The Bucks sent Herro down the other end of the court who took a dribble before getting his three up. He buried the shot, silencing the crowd, but replay showed that he just didn't get it off in time, making the Sixers very lucky to escape this one in regulation.

#2803. Manchester United XI vs Liverpool – Predicted lineup and team news

Manchester United travel to Anfield on Sunday for Mauricio Pochettino's first match as head coach, with the 18th-placed visitors still searching for a first away win.

Pochettino was appointed after Michael Carrick left following a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa that extended United's winless Premier League run to eight matches. United have eight points from 10 games, their worst top-flight start in 40 years, with one win, five draws and four defeats, and are 10 points and 14 places behind Liverpool. They are yet to win away in any competition this season, drawing three and losing four, and the Argentine has not won any of his last eight games against Liverpool in all tournaments, losing six. Eight of United's last nine permanent or caretaker managers avoided defeat in their first game, Erik ten Hag the exception.

Carlos Baleba (hamstring), Manuel Ugarte (knee) and Benjamin Sesko (calf) remain sidelined, while Luke Shaw (muscle) and Marcus Rashford (hip) are reported to be close to returning, though their availability is uncertain. Harry Maguire and Patrick Dorgu trained on Thursday after fitness concerns, with Maguire seemingly recovered from the back injury that forced him off against Villa. Dorgu returned from a muscular injury in the draw with Villa.

Lisandro Martinez is back from suspension and could replace Noussair Mazraoui, the only change expected from the side that started against Villa. Leny Yoro would move to right-back, with Diogo Dalot on the left. Kobbie Mainoo and Youri Tielemans are predicted to stay in midfield, while Bruno Fernandes will remain captain, as Pochettino confirmed at his press conference. Liverpool's Cody Gakpo has scored five Premier League goals against United, his highest total against a single team.

Manchester United possible starting XI: Lammens; Yoro, Martinez, Maguire, Dalot; Tielemans, Mainoo; Diallo, Fernandes, Cunha; Mbeumo

The match kicks off at 16:30 GMT on Sunday, 22 November 2026.

The match will be broadcast live on Sky Sports.

#2802. NFL didn't know Puka Nacua had surgery when agreeing to suspension

The sudden decision of the NFL to suspend receiver Puka Nacua three games raises plenty of relevant questions. On Friday, a technically irrelevant question was answered.

After Rams coach Sean McVay disclosed to reporters that Nacua had surgery to repair a torn MCL suffered during a Week 9 win over the Commanders, Judy Battista of NFL Network reported this: “The NFL did not know Puka Nacua had had knee surgery on Tuesday when it suspended him yesterday.”

Surely, that’s accurate; Judy is a solid reporter. But that extra fact doesn’t matter to the NFL’s ultimate handling of Nacua.

The report creates the impression that, during the negotiations that resulted in the three-game suspension under the Personal Conduct Policy, Nacua and/or his representatives withheld material information. That, at some level, the league had been duped by the lack of knowledge that Nacua had undergone surgery that will sideline him for “a couple months.”

First, the suspension (as the league affirmatively explained on Thursday) is separate from the injury. The NFL listed multiple examples of players who served suspensions while injured.

Second, whether Nacua (who had already missed one of the four minimum games he’s required to miss while on IR), was already going to serve the entirety of the suspension while on injured reserve with or without surgery.

Third, the surgery and the extended absence actually avoids the possibility that Nacua would have returned to action exactly one game after finishing his suspension, resulting in no net games missed due to the suspension.

The mere existence of the report represents an implicit acknowledgement by the league that the negotiated suspension, aimed at allowing Nacua to double-dip when it comes to his stint on injured reserve and his suspension, underscores the broader question of whether players should be allowed to serve a suspension under the Personal Conduct Policy when otherwise unable to play. That’s a separate issue for the NFL to revisit, if it chooses, through a potential revision to the terms of the policy.

As explained on Friday, the league could have refused the deal. It could have insisted on pushing the issue through the full process, with Judge Sue L. Robinson conducting a hearing and the outcome potentially being appealed (by Nacua and/or the league) to the Commissioner. But that would have entailed a non-zero-percent chance that Nacua wouldn’t have been suspended at all.

For Nacua, the goal was to get the suspension served while injured. The fact that he had surgery before the agreement was reached gave him more of a buffer than the NFL knew about, expanding the window during which the suspension could be fully served before the end of the 2026 season without Nacua missing time due to the suspension. Which would still allow Nacua to have this issue behind him before the 2027 offseason, when he signs a new contract with the Rams, has the franchise tag applied, or becomes a free agent.

Of course, Nacua now needs to tread lightly. The league has made it clear that “any further violation of the Personal Conduct Policy, including failing to satisfy the conditions of his diversion program, will subject him to additional and more substantial discipline, including banishment from the League.” To the extent that the league isn’t thrilled by the perception/reality that it allowed Nacua to serve his suspension at a time when he otherwise could not play, the league could be ready to pounce on any opportunity to impose a longer suspension that would apply when Nacua is healthy again.

#2801. AMD dives on short seller report of alleged China exposure

Shares of chipmaker Advanced Micro Devices close down 7.72% to $489.19 on Thursday after investment firm Hunterbrook Media issued a report alleging that the company "has its own China problem."

Hunterbrook disclosed that it had taken a short position in AMD.

The report claims that "after the U.S. blacklisted Chinese server maker IEIT Systems, the company kept getting newer AMD firmware, which enables AMD chips to run in servers."

CNBC did not independently verify this information.

Hunterbrook also said that new export rules in Washington could "tighten restrictions on AMD's foreign business," alleging that AMD "bills over 20% of its revenue to customers in China and Hong Kong," and "depends on a Chinese partner to finish most of its chips."

The allegations come amid investigations of rival chipmaker Nvidia over its sales to companies in Asia with ties to China. AMD has been viewed as a beneficiary of the uncertainty surrounding Nvidia.

"The claims misrepresent AMD's business practices and relationships," an AMD spokesperson said in a statement. "AMD complies with applicable US export control laws and regulations and maintains rigorous processes to ensure compliance across our global operations. We remain focused on serving our customers and upholding the highest standards of integrity."

Lisa Su, CEO of AMD, told investors on the company's recent earnings call that China is an "important" market and that the semiconductor giant wants to be fully compliant with U.S. export controls.

AMD continued to slide after hours, trading down 0.45% to $487 shortly after 4 p.m.

It seems a little early in the season to call your team soft, but Udoka is an old-school, defense-first, outwork-your-opponent kind of coach, and Houston's effort lately is clearly frustrating him. It's a long regular season where the Rockets can work out their defense and get it on point, this is a team that can make a deep run in the West. #2797. Anthropic was supposed to save the AI trade. What went wrong?

Anthropic's historic IPO — the largest in U.S. history — delivered a big win for the company and its investors. But it failed at another task: reviving animal spirits in the AI trade.

The Nasdaq Composite closed down 2.25% amid a broader market pullback with the S&P 500 declining 2% to 7,175.

Investors had been looking forward to the Anthropic IPO as a "clearing event" — reviving the tech sector after months of choppy trading, the Nvidia espionage scandal, and growing concerns that AI chip sales to Asia are likely to slump as Washington cracks down on potential violations of export controls.

Some of those worries resurfaced Thursday as a short seller, Hunterbrook Media, alleged in a report that firmware sold by chipmaker AMD had turned up in U.S.-blacklisted companies. AMD stock closed down more than 7%. The chipmaker told CNBC it complies with U.S. law.

Adding to the worries, the House Foreign Affairs Committee released documents showing that a subagency of the Commerce Department approved the sale of more than 879,000 AI chips to Chinese companies. That also raised the prospect of an Asia sales slowdown under stricter export controls.

Many investors and analysts continue to express confidence in the AI trade, noting that Anthropic's successful market debut — the stock closed up 17% from its IPO price and raised more than $81 billion — bodes well for capital spending across tech.

But investors acknowledged that this is a high-tension moment and said it's time to be more strategic about the trade.

Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management, described himself as a "circumspect bull," making the comparison to market conditions in 2022 when the big indices tanked in the back half of the year.

"You could have still made money that year. You just needed to have the right combination of longs and shorts," he said.

Part of the challenge, Niles said, has been a price war between the two cutting-edge frontier labs, Anthropic and OpenAI. That has posed a threat to the overall revenue and profit potential of AI and the capex going into it. Fierce competition on price is producing collective losses between the two top companies, he notes.

"The combined revenue growth for OpenAI plus Anthropic went from growing 75% sequentially in Q2 to 48% in Q3," he told CNBC. "The combined losses went from $7.9 billion to about $8.4 billion from Q2 to Q3."

While uncertainty is high now, some resolution may be on the horizon if a greater degree of commercial and regulatory clarity emerges over the next few months.

Trading experts also told CNBC that the tech sector's troubles Thursday were a more serious matter than Anthropic simply siphoning capital from other parts of the market.

"There's a reset of AI in general," said JJ Kinahan, senior vice president at Cboe Global Markets.

Investors for years have profited in tech by buying the dip, he said, but "there's been an attitude change that [you] can't buy things blindly and they'll all go up." Nvidia's ongoing declines have shaken confidence in the buy-the-dip strategy, he said.

Anthropic's next few trading days will be a key gauge of the market's strength. Friday will be a "wild card," Kinahan said. It could be a "final flush out" for the market, or a bounce that leaves the market in "no man's land" while volatility remains elevated.

A better gauge may be Monday, when options on Anthropic start trading, providing insight into whether retail investors will back the company in force — providing more of a vote of confidence in the AI trade.

"If people think we're going up [on Anthropic] they'll buy stock, but retail particularly will just buy calls," he said. "There's a bigger test coming Monday."

For now, investors continue to sell AI-related stocks.

Neoclouds, the generally highly indebted AI-dedicated computing providers at the outer edges of the AI trade, were down substantially Thursday, with CoreWeave closing down more than 6%.

Hyperscalers such as Alphabet and Amazon.com, viewed as relatively defensive AI bets, also finished lower.

One bright spot: Cybersecurity stocks continued to rally, with Palo Alto Networks and CrowdStrike both closing up. AI is finding security holes across software infrastructure, creating a scramble to patch the vulnerabilities before hackers can exploit them.

— CNBC's Daren Fonda contributed reporting.

#2795. Has Bo Nix quieted early-season criticisms ahead of Las Vegas Raiders-Denver Broncos Week 11 matchup?

Week 11 reignites a longstanding AFC West rivalry between the Las Vegas Raiders and Denver Broncos. To preview the game, Silver and Black Pride reached out to our frenemies at Mile High Report, and Ian St. Clair was kind enough to answer a few questions about the Broncos for Raider Nation.

Q: Bo Nix faced a lot of criticism at the beginning of the season for not pushing the ball down the field. Has that changed over the last handful of games? Is ending last season with a broken ankle impacting how he's been playing in 2026?

A: No matter what happens on the field, Nix is one of those players who will always face criticism. It's also important to add some context for why things may unfold on the field over the course of a game. Early in the season, Nix and the Broncos offense played some of the best defenses in the NFL. On top of that, Denver's running game was horrendous, and that's even putting it mildly.

Opposing defenses knew they could stop the Broncos rushing attack with minimal effort and play two-high safeties. In situations like that, it's going to be hard for a quarterback or offense to push the ball down the field. However, Nix and Denver did open it up a bit more against the Carolina Panthers. Early in the game, Nix took some deep shots that set up the offense for points. We will likely see a continuation of that this Sunday.

As for Nix's injury, there's no doubt it impacted him earlier in the season. I'd say more so mentally than physically. All the physical rehab in the world doesn't rehab the mind until you actually do it and prove to yourself that you can do it. The last few games have been more like the Nix we saw before the injury, and belief and confidence are reemerging.

Q: On a related note, Jaylen Waddle was the Broncos' biggest offseason addition but has just 30 catches for 337 yards and two touchdowns through nine games. What were the expectations for Waddle heading into the season, and what's been holding him back from meeting them?

A: The expectations were high that Waddle would bring an element to Denver's offense that would open it up. Nix and the Broncos would be able to take deep shots and use his speed in the middle of the field. It's been surprising how inconsistent that has been thus far. We've seen flashes, but not nearly enough. Obviously, what I mentioned earlier with the focus opposing defenses can put on the passing game plays into that, but it's also not utilizing him properly. Plus, the fact that Nix has been inconsistent and struggled hasn't helped. Coming out of the bye, I think we'll start to see Waddle play a bigger role in the Broncos offense.

Q: Who would you say is more important to Denver's defense and why: Nik Bonitto or Pat Surtain II?

A: My answer might change in five minutes, but I'll go with Bonitto. Mind you, I think that Surtain is the best cornerback in football. He's one of those cornerbacks who shuts down half the field. However, having a game-changing edge rusher in Bonitto sets the rest of the defense up for success, including Surtain.

What Bonitto brings and how effective he is reminds me of Von Miller, who was the Super Bowl 50 MVP. At any moment, Bonitto can make a play that changes a game. Or, in the case against the Panthers, win it. He's become one of the best edge players in the league, and he just brings so much to the Broncos' defense.

Q: Seahawks head coach/defensive play caller Mike Macdonald gave Fernando Mendoza trouble last week by throwing the book at the rookie with several exotic pressure looks. Should Mendoza expect the same from Vance Joseph this week? What can you tell us about Joseph's scheme that could give a quarterback making his second career start fits?

A: The scheme is only as good as the players who execute it, and that's where I think Mendoza will face similar challenges this Sunday. Denver is one of the best defenses in the NFL, if not the best, at pressuring the quarterback. Even if Joseph doesn't call a blitz, the Broncos front will still make life miserable for Mendoza with Bonitto, Jonathan Cooper, Zack Allen, and Co.

But then there are the times Joseph will send a cornerback or safety on a blitz out of package that Mendoza isn't expecting. But the biggest reason Mendoza will face pressure is the Raiders' currently ineffective running game. Being one-dimensional on offense is never a friend to a rookie quarterback making his second start against a great defense.

Q: Can you give us one player on each side of the ball who Raider Nation might not know much about but could make an impact in this game? A couple of the Broncos' up-and-comers, if you will.

A: On offense, let's go with rookie running back Jonah Coleman. Though I'm pretty sure Las Vegas fans know of Coleman. But he is what makes Denver's running game click. Coleman's has good vision, good speed, and runs with power. He's been fun to watch, and I would expect him to get more carries on Sunday.

On defense, there really isn't anybody who Raiders Nation hasn't heard of. So I'll go with cornerback Ja'Quan McMillian. The fourth-year undrafted defensive back has a knack for making a key play when Denver needs it, whether an interception or a sack.

#2791. No. 5 Texas vs. Arkansas final score: Horns blow out Hogs in 45-3 win

It's Senior Day on the Forty Acres as quarterback Arch Manning plans to participate in what could be his final regular-season home game at Darrell K Royal-Texas Memorial Stadium as the No. 5 Texas Longhorns host the Arkansas Razorbacks.

An SEC championship game and College Football Playoff berth are on the line for Steve Sarkisian's team, which lost its margin for error with back-to-back losses to unranked opponents in October before surviving a home test against then-No. 7 Ole Miss and road challenges at then-No. 18 Missouri and then-No. 21 LSU.

Now Texas is hoping for the type of comfortable victory that has eluded the team in SEC play. At 2-5 in the conference, Arkansas represents a prime opportunity for one, although the Hogs proved how dangerous they can be in taking advantage of an injury to Gamecock quarterback LaNorris Seller to beat then-No. 17 South Carolina 34-6 last week.

Listed as questionable on the first three SEC-mandated player availability reports, Texas junior edge Colin Simmons was ruled out on Saturday in the report released 90 minutes before kickoff.

Senior cornerback Warren Roberson was upgraded from probable to available, while sixth-year offensive guard Laurence Seymore is a game-time decision.

Arkansas won the toss and elected to receive the start the game. After a touchback, a holding penalty drawn by Texas sophomore defensive end Lance Jackson put Arkansas behind the chains, but a positive run and completion put the Razorbacks in 3rd and 4. Initially ruled a completion at the sticks as KJ Jackson got the throw off under pressure, but review overturned the call to force a punt by Arkansas.

A short punt led to Ryan Niblett getting hit trying to field the ball, a 15-yard penalty that allowed Texas to start its first possession just short of midfield. Manning opened the game throwing it, completing a 16-yard pass to junior wide receiver Cam Coleman, a shorter pass to junior wide receiver Emmett Mosley V that went for five yards, and a screen to junior Ryan Wingo that was going to force Texas to convert a third down, but instead resulted a hands to the face penalty on the perimeter that produced another 15 yards in penalty yardage for the Longhorns.

Manning kept it on a zone read, but lost a yard as the play that stretched. A push pass to Wingo worked better, as the speedy wide receiver was able to take the edge, cut upfield, and pick up 12 yards to the 3-yard line. Trying to throw a play-action pass with Manning rolling to his right was unsuccessful, as the Texas quarterback had to throw the ball away. In a two-back formation, Manning kept a zone read and followed blocks by Mosley and fifth-year tight end Michael Masunas for a three-yard touchdown run and a 7-0 lead.

A tough run on first down by Sutton Smith gained four yards, but his second-down effort was stopped from the back side by senior defensive end Colton Vasek. On 3rd and 6, Jackson's intended receiver bobbled the catch and senior safety Jelani McDonald was in the right place at the right time, coming up with the interception and seeing nothing but open field in front of him on a 31-yard return and a 14-0 lead, bolstering McDonald's late-season Jim Thorpe Award campaign.

The interception was McDonald's fourth in the last two games.

After a first-down incompletion, KJ Jackson rumbled up the middle for a 12-yard gain. Smith ran for five yards on the next play, then Jackson connected with Chris Marshall up the sideline, and Marshall was able to make Roberson miss to gain 28 yards. Three runs by Cam Settles picked up another first down, including a big hole created on 3rd and 7 for seven yards. On first down, however, Sutton was stuffed, and Jackson had to settle for an underneath completion on second down for four yards. Facing 3rd and 6, Jackson tried to target Marshall on a slant, but cornerback Kade Phillips had sticky coverage, and Arkansas settled for a 30-yard field goal.

The next Texas possession fizzled out quickly after a run and catch by Brown — his third-down effort was blown up behind the line of scrimmage, and the punt unit committed a delay of game penalty.

A good play by sophomore defensive lineman Justus Terry stopped Arkansas for a two-yard run on first down, but a big hole in the Texas zone coverage afforded Jackson an easy completion over the middle for 16 yards. Jackson opted to snap it before the first quarter ended and it almost resulted in a disaster as Jackson won with an inside move and forced a fumble recovered by the Razorbacks.

On 3rd and 11, the Longhorns only rushed four, and while Jackson was able to get out of the pocket to his right without any pressure, the coverage held long enough to force Jackson to take a five-yard gain and the Arkansas punt unit to take the field, pinning Texas at its 5-yard line on a fair catch by Niblett.

A sixth-yard run by Terry create some space before Mosley found a seam on a screen pass to the boundary behind good blocking, including from senior right tackle Adnre Cojoe, for a 41-yard gain. Another explosive play was produced by the individual effort of Bishop, who spun out of a tackle attempt and ran through another 18 yards.

Wingo ran for five yards on a reverse with a nice upfield cut and Terry took three carries for 14 yards, but Manning couldn't hit Coleman in the end zone and then took a sack on third down when Cojoe got knocked backwards by a defensive tackle who was mostly just trying to set up a twist for his defensive end. A 37-yard field goal by senior Gianni Spetic made it 17-3.

Texas gave up a chunk play on a wheel route by Breylon Russell that either wasn't carried up the sideline or didn't have a safety get over the top of it, gaining 23 yards. A catch and run by Marshall went for 13 yards after Russell was stuffed. Sophomore nickel back Graceson Littleton delivered a big hit on Settles on the next play, but it came at the end of a seven-yard gain. Hoping for undisciplined play by Texas, Arkansas called an end around, but the Longhorns played it well for no gain, Jackson was stopped for a one-yard gain on third down, and then threw incomplete on fourth down, a drop by Russell after Jackson rolled to his left.

After taking over on its own 30-yard line, Manning hit Mosley on an arrow route for four yards, Brown showed some patience and leg drive to gain four yards up the middle, and Manning bounced a designed run behind effective blocks by Masunas, Wingo, and Coleman for 10 yards. A screen to Wingo only picked up two yards, but Manning hung in the pocket long enough to throw a dime to Mosley on a corner route for 29 yards, but the defender punched at the ball enough to force a review that upheld the call on the field.

Working from a clean pocket, Manning delivered another accurate throw, this time to sophomore tight end Emaree Winston, who ran a post route behind a corner by Mosley that put the safety in conflict — when the safety took Mosley, Winston was wide open for a 21-yard touchdown, the first of his career, as the linebacker .

Winston's touchdown catch put Texas up 24-3.

At the two-minute timeout, Arkansas had a nine-yard reception upheld on review, setting up 2nd and 1 from its 34-yard line. Two chunk completions moved the ball well into Texas territory, where the Longhorns held, sacking Jefferson on 3rd and 6 to force a 44-yard field goal attempt that missed wide left.

Trying to score before halftime, Wingo got open for a 29-yard reception and a 22-yard catch that showed off his ability to extend outside the framework of his body. When Bishop got out of bounds on a short reception to stop the clock, Sarkisian dialed up a fake swing pass to Brown that turned into a tunnel screen, gashing Arkansas up the middle as three offensive linemen struggled to find work, allowing Mosley to score from 17 yards out and extending the lead to 31-3.

Unfortunately for Texas, a late-half throw by Jackson resulted in a knee injury sustained by Longhorns junior safety Kobe Black, who had to be helped off the field before the final two plays of the half.

To open the second half, a screen pass to Brown picked up 18 yards, but to extend the drive, Manning had to vacate the pocket on 3rd and 11 when Terry missed his assignment in blitz pickup, but was able to find Mosley over the middle. Two short runs to freshman running back Derrek Cooper forced the Horns to convert a 3rd and 6, another completion to Mosley, who went out of bounds just short of the line to gain. Facing 4th and 1, Terry took a carry up the middle and drove his legs through contact, picking up eight yards.

Dialing up a jet reverse flea flicker throwback screen, Brown worked behind some effective blocks and navigated a lot of traffic down to the goal line, but didn't quite have enough juice to finish it.

After the play, Cojoe needed medical attention on the field for a possible cramp. Cojoe was able to walk off the field under his own power.

In the team's jumbo package, Terry had an easy walk into the end zone behind freshman tight end Charlie Jilek, taking over for Winston as the short-yardage fullback. It was Jilek's first collegiate snap.

The first-team Texas defense remained on the field as Arkansas moved the ball steadily until the Longhorns were able to force a 3rd and 9, an incompletion forced by senior cornerback Bo Mascoe with physical coverage. Staying on the field, the Razorbacks nearly pulled off a big passing play down near the goal line that was ruled incomplete.

Surprisingly, the play wasn't reviewed, and Texas took over with Manning remaining in the game, handing it off to Brown, who had a nice crease to exploit for eight yards on first down. Hurried on second down after an injury timeout, Manning threw a rare incompletion, but Terry was able to convert in short yardage again.

On 3rd and 16, Manning scrambled for 13 yards, allowing the offense to stay on the field to try to move the chains. Working from a bunch formation, Winston made a good catch outside his framework for a 24-yard gain, but the sophomore tight end was called for offensive pass interference on a questionable call that resulted in a punt that bounced into the end zone.

The defense started to work some backups in on the Arkansas drive, including junior defensive back Xavier Filsaime making his season debut. Seventh-year linebacker Darius Snow also saw playing time on defense for the first time since the season opener. The group played well enough to hold Arkansas on 4th and 6, allowing the Longhorns to take over at their own 33-yard line.

Manning departed with about 12 minutes left in the fourth quarter as sophomore KJ Lacey completed first first attempt to Bishop and Cooper gained four yards. Needing to convert 3rd and 2, Lacey checked down to Cooper, who picked up the first down behind a block from Winston. Looking for a shot play, Lacey took a hit while delivering a strike to fifth-year wide receiver Sterling Berkhalter, who made the catch at full stretch, gaining 35 yards.

A good run off the edge by Cooper gained five yards; another went for four. On 3rd and 1, it took a second effort by Cooper to secure the yard to gain. Still running well, Cooper had a clean hole, running through a defender to stretch for the goal line on the eight-yard touchdown run to make it 45-3.

On Senior Day, senior linebacker Brady Sarkisian got in on kickoff coverage, his second snap of the season.

With the Arkansas backup quarterback in the game, Marshall made a 14-yard reception on 3rd and 10, but a holding call eventually put them behind the chains, a situation that sophomore safety Jonah Williams took advantage of with a tackle in the flat. Trying to throw it on 3rd and 8, Hank Hendrix was sacked by the turf monster. The Razorbacks went for it on 4th and 18 and freshman cornerback John Meredith came up with his first career interception near the goal line, producing a gift for Bevo on the other side of the field.

Emptying the bench of Jabbar Juluke's running back room, sophomore James Simon received five carries to end the game at 45-3.

#2790. Tyler Herro scores 50, officials miss late out-of-bounds call on Joel Embiid in 76ers' 132-129 win over Bucks

Tyler Herro scored a career-high 50 points Saturday, but that wasn't enough for the Milwaukee Bucks to get their second win of the season in a 132-129 defeat to the Philadelphia 76ers.

Milwaukee was deprived of an opportunity to take the lead in the closing seconds when officials missed Joel Embiid stepping out of bounds.

Rookie Brayden Burries hit a corner 3-pointer for the Bucks to cut the Sixers' lead to 130-129. After a timeout, LeBron James threw an inbounds pass to Embiid, who attempted to dribble out the clock near the Milwaukee basket. Embiid stepped out of bounds at the baseline with his left foot, but the officials did not call the turnover and Herro was forced to foul.

The Bucks could not challenge the non-call because they were out of replay challenges.

Embiid made his two free throws to put Philadelphia ahead by 3, but the Bucks still had 1.5 seconds remaining to try for a tying basket.

Herro received the inbounds pass from Kyle Kuzma at the top of the arc and took one dribble to break free from VJ Edgecombe. Shooting from the right elbow, Herro's 3-pointer went in. However, the shot left his hand after the buzzer.

The eighth-year guard shot 15 for 23 from the floor, including 7 of 13 on 3-pointers. Herro also hit all 11 of his free throws, adding 11 rebounds and seven assists. Burries finished with 26 points off the bench with 8 rebounds and four assists. Kuzma was the third Bucks player to reach double figures, scoring 12 with five boards.

Herro's previous career-high was 41 points, reached in a December 2022 win over the Houston Rockets while he was with the Miami Heat.

Milwaukee lost its 13th consecutive game to drop to 1-16 on the season, the worst mark in the NBA. The Bucks' only win came nearly a month ago on Oct. 26, a 110-102 victory over the Indiana Pacers. Up next for them is the Toronto Raptors (6-10) at home on Monday.

Embiid led the Sixers with 26 points, nearly registering a triple-double with 17 rebounds and nine assists. James added 21 points with 6 boards, 6 assists and 3 steals. Seven Philadelphia players scored in double figures, with Anfernee Simons getting 18 off the bench and Tyrese Maxey tallying 17 points.

The 76ers improved to 14-3 with the win, tying the Los Angeles Lakers for the NBA's top record. Philadelphia has won five consecutive games and next faces the Portland Trail Blazers (10-5) on Monday.

#2789. Hull City vs Brighton – Predicted lineup and team news

Hull City host second-placed Brighton at the MKM Stadium on Sunday, looking to end a winless run that dates back to late August.

Sergej Jakirovic's side sit 13th in the Premier League with 11 points, three clear of the relegation zone, after winning their opening two matches against Manchester United and Coventry City. They have since gone eight league matches without a win, and nine in all competitions, though they remain one of four sides yet to lose at home this season. Brighton arrive second, five points behind Arsenal, as the top flight's leading scorers, with six clean sheets from their last seven league matches. Sunday brings the clubs' first Premier League meeting, their last meeting of any kind a 0-0 Championship draw in February 2016.

Matt Targett (ankle), Hidemasa Morita (muscle), Eliot Matazo (knee) and Ilyas Ansah (back) are injured, while Nobel Mendy (knee), Mohamed Belloumi (thigh) and Charlie Hughes (groin) are doubtful. Matt Crooks is back in contention after serving a one-match suspension against Arsenal for accumulating yellow cards. Hull have kept all three of their league defeats narrow, losing 2-1 at Newcastle United and 1-0 at both Fulham and Arsenal.

Belloumi leads Hull's scoring charts with three goals and five direct goal involvements, underlining the importance of his possible recovery. Oli McBurnie has added two goals and two assists and offers Jakirovic another potential match-winner. Hull face a Brighton side whose Pascal Gross has four goals and four assists this term, and Charalampos Kostoulas and Maxim De Cuyper each have five goal involvements for the visitors. Pascal Struijk, Chema Andres and Jaouen Hadjam could return for Brighton, whose absentees include Kaoru Mitoma, Evan Ferguson, Stefanos Tzimas and Zadok Yohanna.

#2788. Will Shane Steichen be back in 2027?

After the 2025 season ended, Colts owner Carlie Irsay-Gordon put both G.M. Chris Ballard and coach Shane Steichen on notice that much was expected in 2026.

Then, one day after the Colts lost to the Titans in Week 6, she fired Ballard and kept Steichen.

Nearly five weeks later, Steichen remains. After next Sunday’s game against the Giants, the Colts will enter their bye. That’s typically the best time for an in-season coaching change, since it gives the interim coach maximum time to get up to speed before the next game.

Creating a vacancy with six weeks left in the regular season carries a separate benefit. The team can get a head start on searching for the replacement. And if ownership already knows the coach will be fired after the season ends, it makes no sense to delay that part of the process.

So here’s the question. Will Irsay-Gordon bring Steichen back in 2027?

It makes no sense to keep a new G.M. from hiring a new coach. Every G.M. arrives with a wish list of coaches who would be pursued, if/when the position is vacant. It makes plenty of sense to give a new G.M. an immediate vacancy to fill.

But what about the Colts this year has made sense? The firing of the G.M. left the team without a seasoned, experienced executive to navigate the strategy of making, or not making, trades. They quickly moved guard Quenton Nelson and defensive tackle DeForest Buckner. They rejected offers for running back Jonathan Taylor and quarterback Daniel Jones. They moved two starting receivers — Keenan Allen and Josh Downs — on the day of the trade deadline.

Steichen has been operating for much of the season with one hand tied behind his back. Now, he’ll have both wrists secured. Whatever the decision will be as to Steichen, it’s unfair to base it on the team’s overall performance since the Nelson and Buckner trades.

Before that point, they took the Chiefs to overtime in Kansas City. They beat the Texans. They manhandled the Commanders in London. They took the Vikings to the limit in Minnesota.

Although the signs point to Steichen eventually being fired, the fact that he’s still there now means he could still be there later. The new G.M. may be expected to work with him.

It’s ultimately the owner’s call. And the owner may have strong feelings about Steichen’s skills and abilities, based on the fact that she’s on the sideline every week.

If she was upstairs, she’d likely be hearing criticism of the head coach during the game. Instead, she’s watching him in his element. And she hasn’t fired him yet.

Throw in the fact that a new coach may not be as welcoming to the owner’s presence in the bench area, and she may be even more inclined to give Steichen (who’s fine with it) another chance. Especially since she systematically began making his job much more difficult the moment the Colts traded Nelson and Buckner.

Moving forward, it’ll be virtually impossible. The fact that Steichen has survived this long could mean that he’ll survive whatever comes next.

#2787. 49ers’ Kaelon Black manifested NFL future with Kyle Shanahan

Kaelon Black is making a name for himself by putting together a strong rookie year for the 49ers, proving to be a perfect supporting piece to Christian McCaffrey.

Black was the lead back for the national champion Indiana Hoosiers last year, but he always had his mind on one thing.

49ers running back Kaelon Black celebrating with his teammates in an NFL game. AP Photo/Jeffrey McWhorter

Playing in the NFL and playing for a Shanahan.

On Thursday, he appeared on the NFL Network show "Good Morning Football" and told host Jamie Erdahl that he wrote in a book as a kid that he would play in the NFL under a Shanahan.

"It's funny because I did," Black said. "I was probably in like fourth grade in Washington at the time, and at the time, Mike Shanahan was the coach and I was saying, like, 'I'm going to be the first overall pick drafted by Mike Shanahan. I'm going to play with [Robert Griffin III]' and all this other stuff. I was one of those kids who just dreamed big, and I knew one day that I was going to be in the NFL."

While Black had to watch former Hoosiers teammate Fernando Mendoza be selected with the No. 1 overall pick, the 25-year-old running back was taken by San Francisco in the third round and has found himself a role in the NFL.

This year, Black has 79 carries for 356 yards and two touchdowns — both happened over the 49ers' last three games.

In last week's 46-40 win against the Cowboys, Black had one of his most impressive runs of his young NFL career, when he ran a 25-yard touchdown down the field and started it by running through Caleb Downs, the No. 11 overall pick in the 2026 draft, when he lowered his shoulder on the Ohio State product.

Kaelon Black running downfield in an NFL game against the Cowboys. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Kaelon Black running through Cowboys cornerback Cobie Durant. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Black then got himself back into position and immediately shoved Cowboys cornerback Cobie Durant to the ground to finish the play.

Black's championship-caliber DNA has transitioned to the NFL. The 49ers enter Week 11 with an 8-1 record — the NFC's best — and appear to be true Super Bowl contenders.

#2786. Rapid Recap: 76ers 132, Bucks 129

The best team in the East, the Philadelphia 76ers, beat the reeling Milwaukee Bucks, 132-129. The win means the 76ers increase their win streak to five, while the loss means the Bucks increase their losing streak to 13. Despite the loss, it was a valiant effort by the Bucks, with Tyler Herro scoring a career-high 50 points and Brayden Burries posting a career-high 26, but Joel Embiid's 26-point, 17-rebound double-double proved too much.

Following LeBron starting the game with a vintage hammer, Tyler Herro (going back to the headband) quickly gave the Bucks their first points with a three. This back-and-forth from the stars continued for a bit, until Herro took the upper hand going into the first stoppage in the first quarter, at 6:24, with another three—the Bucks holding a 15-8 advantage. This seven-point advantage got trimmed to one, with the 76ers getting what they desired in the paint, taking a 21-20 lead. Although Jabari Walker started to get in on the paint punishment for the Sixers, Kel'el Ware jams and Herro's deep threat weathered the storm. The Bucks took a 30-29 lead into the second quarter, on the shoulders of Headband Herro's 15 points.

At the second's first stoppage, at 6:41, the game remained close but in Milwaukee's hands, 43-40. After being a game-time decision and held quiet early, Tyrese Maxey showed up in the second quarter, scoring six points in a little over three minutes; however, Herro remained hot to neutralise him—20 points with 2:34 remaining in the half! An and-one late in the quarter for Joel Embiid would give the Sixers a 60-59 lead going into the break.

Again, Herro gave the Bucks their first bucket of the half, and Ryan Rollins followed that up with a fast-break lay. But LeBron continued to exploit the Bucks' struggling interior defense with a one-handed slam (even if he showed his age—this was no signature tomahawk). While the Bucks took a 68-63 lead, they lost it before the third's first break at 7:58, going down 70-71. Herro tried to counter Embiid's quick double-double, but between Kyle Kuzma shooting below 30% from the field and Myles Turner hucking threes at a 0/5 clip, the game began to slip away. 83-73 Sixers. Or was it? Herro's 35 after three had the Bucks within striking distance, down just six, 92-98, going into the fourth.

With five unanswered points less than two minutes into the quarter, Barlow forced the Bucks into a quick timeout. 103-92 Sixers. However, Headband Herro wouldn't quit! He'd have a 40-ball with eight minutes left. This, in turn, forced the Sixers into a timeout, as their lead was cut to three, 106-103. The end of the game went blow for blow, Herro and Brayden Burries vs. Embiid and LeBron, but age, a missed out-of-bounds call, and a waived-off buzzer-beater helped the Sixers prevail. What a heartbreaker, but what a game!

Tyler Herro scored 50 points on 15/23 shooting! While it didn't give the Bucks the win, it kept them within fighting distance of the top team in the East. It also could have been more if his game-tying buzzer-beater wasn't called off.

Split Myles Turner's minutes amongst Kel'el Ware and Jericho Sims. At least they grab boards and have awareness of their three-point capabilities.
